# Demonstration XXIII on the Grape (Demonstratio XXIII de Acino)

**Author**: Aphraates  
**Series**: PO Tome PS  
**Columns**: pp. 2–150  

[p. 2] {#po.0.p2}
ܬܚܘܝܬܐ ܟܓ ܥܠ ܣܓܘܠܐ
DEMONSTRATION XXIII CONCERNING THE GRAPE
ܐܶܟܬܘܽܒ ܘܐܶܚܰܘܶܝܟ܂ ܚܰܒܺܝܒܝ ܪܰܚܺܝܡܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܗܳܠܶܝܢ ܪ̈ܺܝܫܶܐ ܕܰܐܒܰܥܬܰܢܝ: ܥܰܠ ܗܳܝ ܒܘܽܪܟܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܰܛܡܝܺܪܳܐ ܒܰܣܓܘܽܠܳܐ: ܕ݁ܡܶܛܽܠ ܛܘܽܦܣܳܬܳܐ ܕܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܠܳܐ ܐܶܬܚܰܒܰܠ ܣܓܘܽܠܳܐ ܠܰܚܒܳܠܬܳܐ. ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܰܒܚܰܘܡܰܫ ܐܶܫܰܥܝܳܐ ܨܳܒܶܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܡܳܪܶܗ ܕܰܣܓ݁ܘܽܠܳܐ ܕ݁ܰܢܚܰܒܠܝܽܘܗ̱ܝ: ܘܐܶܬܶܐܡܰܪ ܠܶܗ ܒܦܶܬ݂ܓ݂ܳܡܳܐ ܕܠܳܐ ܢܶܬܚܰܒܰܠ ܣܓܘܽܠܳܐ ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܐܺܝܬ ܒܶܗ. ܘܰܕܟܺܝܠܬ݁ܳܢܝ ܬܘܽܒ: ܕܐܶܢ ܒܟܽܠܙܒ݁ܰܢ ܫܰܟܺܝܚܺܝܢ ܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܘܟܺܐ݀ܢܶܐ ܥܰܠ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ: ܘܐܰܦܝܺܣܳܟ݂ ܥܰܠ ܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܗܳܝ ܕܰܪܕܳܐ ܒܟܽܠܗܘܰܢ ܕܳܪ̈ܶܐ ܠܕ݂ܰܡܛܳܐ ܠܙܰܒܢܳܗ: ܘܐܶܚܰܘܶܝܟ ܐܳܦ ܥܰܠ ܡܶܠܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܰܐܒܰܥܬܳܢܝ: ܕ݁ܰܠܡܳܢܳܝܐ ܒܰܝܙܒܰܢ ܐܶܬܡܰܥܘ ܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܟ݁ܰܕ ܡܨܰܠܶܝܢ ܗ̱ܘܰܘ: ܘܰܒܙܰܒܢܳܐ ܐ̱ܚܪܳܢܐ ܠܳܐ
1. I will write to you, most dear friend, that I may instruct you concerning these chapters which you have proposed to me, namely, concerning the blessing hidden in the cluster; since on account of the blessed grain, the cluster was not given to destruction. For in the days of Isaiah, the lord of the cluster wanted to destroy it; but it was said to the prophet in the discourse that the cluster would not be corrupted, since the blessing is in it. Because you also asked me whether the just and upright will always be upon the earth, I will teach you concerning that blessing, which flowed into all generations until the appointed time, and furthermore I will institute for you a demonstration concerning the discourse about which you questioned me, why sometimes the just were heard when they prayed, why indeed sometimes God did not hear them. Finally, I will describe to you the years of the ancient generations, making a calculation of each,
ܫܡܰܥ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ. ܘܐܶܟܬܘّܒ ܠܳܟ ܐܳܦ ܥܰܠ ܦܘܽܢ̈ܩܶܐ ܕ݁ܳܪ̈ܶܐ ܩܰܕ݁̈ܡܳܝܶܐ ܕܕܳܪ ܕܪ ܚܘܽܫܒܳܢܶܗ ܥܕ݂ܰܡܳܐ ܠܙܰܒܢܳܐ ܕܚܶܪܒ݁ܰܬ ܐܘܽܪܺܝܫܠܶܝܡ܂ ܘܐܶܫܬܰܩܠܰܬ ܡܶܢܶܝܗ ܛܰܝܒܘܽܬܳܐ: ܘܕܰܐܝܟܰܢܳܐ ܙܗܝܺܪܺܝܢ ܗ̱ܘܰܘ ܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܒܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܕܰܪܕܳܬ ܡܶܢܗܘܽܢ ܥܕ݂ܰܡܳܐ ܠܙܰܒܢܳܐ ܕ݁ܰܡܛܳܝܐ ܝܘܽܒܳܠܳܐ. ܒܝܶܫܘܽܥ ܠܥܰܡ̈ܡܶܐ. ܒܪܰܡ܂ ܕܶܝܢ ܥܘܰܩܳܒܳܐ ܕܡܶܠܶܐ ܫܰܦܺܝܪ ܠܡܰܐܠܰܦ ܘܰܠܥܶܡܣܬܰܟܰܠܘܽ: ܐܶܠܶܐ ܝܰܬܝܺܪܳܐܝܺܬ ܒܠܳܒܳܐ ܕܰܕܟܝܐ ܠܡܶܕ݂ܚܰܠ ܡܶܢ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܝܳܗܘܽܒ݁ܳܐ ܕܦ݂ܶܬ݂ܓ݂ܳܡܶܐ ܙܳܕܶܩ ܠܰܢ ܕܰܟܬܰܒ݂ ܘܣܳܡ ܩܕ݂ܳܡܰܝܢ ܕ݁ܰܒ݁ܥܰܡܠܳܐ ܪܰܒ݁ܳܐ ܘܰܒܕܶܚܠܬܳܐ ܣܰܓ݁ܺܝܐܬ݂ܳܐ ܘܰܒܬ݁ܰܪܥܳܐ ܐܰܠܺܝܨܳܐ ܘܰܒܐܘܽܪܚܳܐ ܕܩܰܛܺܝܢܳܐ ܪܳܕܶܝܢ ܕܳܚܠܰܘܗ̱ܝ̈ ܕܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ܀
until the time when Jerusalem was overturned and lost grace; [showing] with what diligence the just preserved the blessing flowing from them, until it was transferred to the nations through Jesus. Indeed, the investigation of words helps much for learning and understanding; but it is more excellent for us to fear God in the purity of the heart, the giver of words, who wrote and proposed to us through great labor and great fear, through the narrow gate and through the strait way, that those who fear God ought to enter.
ܠܘܽܩܕܰܡ ܚܰܒܺܝܒܝ ܘܳܠܶܐ ܠܰܢ ܕܢܶܕ݂ܚܰܠ ܡܶܢ ܕܝܰܢ̈ܐ ܕܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܘܡܶܢ ܡܶܠܰܘܗ̱ܝ̈ ܘܦܘܽܩܕܳܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܕܡܰܚܝܳܢܳܐ ܕܐܶܡܰܪ: ܕܟܰܠ ܡܺܠܳܐ ܒ݁ܰܛܳܠܬܳܐ ܕܢܺܐܡܪܘܽܢ ܒܢܰܝ̈ܢܳܫܳܐ ܢܶܬܠܘܽܢ ܦ݁ܶܬ݂ܓ݂ܳܡܳܐ ܒ݁ܝܰܘܡܳܐ ܕܕܝܺܢܳܐ. ܘܬܘܒ ܟܬܝܺܒ: ܕ݁ܡܶܢ ܡܶܠܰܝ̈ܟ ܬ݁ܶܙܕܰܕܰܩ: ܘܡܶܢ ܡܶܠܰܝ̈ܟ ܬܶܬܚܰܝܰܒ. ܘܬܘܒ ܟܬܝܺܒ: ܕܦܘܽܡܗܘܽܢ ܕܰܒܢܰܝ̈ܢܳܫܳܐ ܪܳܡܶܐ ܠܗܘܽܢ. ܘܰܒܕܘܽܟ݂ܬ݁ܳܐ ܐ̱ܚܪܺܬܳܐ ܟܬܝܺܒ: ܕܦܘܽܡܶܗ ܕܣܰܟܠܳܐ ܥܳܒܶܕ݂ ܠܶܗ ܬܘܽܩܠܬܳܐ. ܘܐܶܬܕܰܟܰܪ ܠܡܶܡܠܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܕܡܰܠܦܳܢܳܐ ܕ݁ܥܰܡ̈ܡܶܐ ܕܐܶܡܰܪ: ܕ݁ܰܠܟ݁ܽܠܰܢ ܠܰܡܩܳܡ ܠܰܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܒܺܝܡ ܕܰܡܫܝܺܚܳܐ ܕܢܶܬܦܪܰܥ ܐ̱ܢܳܫ ܐ݈ܢܳܫ ܒܦܰܓܪܶܗ ܡܶܕܶܡ ܕܡܶܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܥܒܰܕ
2. In the first place it behooves us, dearest, to fear the judgment of God, and the words and precepts of the Vivifier, who says: Every idle word that men have spoken, they will render an account of it on the day of judgment. Again it is written: By your words you will be justified, and by your words you will be condemned. It is also written: The mouth of men casts them down. And in another place it is written: The mouth of the fool prepares a snare for him. Remember also the words of the Teacher of the nations saying: We must all stand before the tribunal of Christ, that each one may be repaid in his body whatever has been done by him before, whether good or evil. He says further: With fear and trembling keep your life.
ܗ݈ܘܳܐ ܠܶܗ ܐܶܢ ܕܛܳܒ ܘܐܶܢ ܕܒ݂ܺܝܫ. ܘܬܘܒ ܐܶܡܰܪ: ܕܰܒܕ݂ܶܚܠܬ݁ܳܐ ܘܒܰܪܬܺܝܬܳܐ ܚܰܝܰܝ̈ܟܘܽܢ ܫܰܘܙܶܒܘ. ܘܬܘܒ ܐܰܘܣܶܦ ܘܙܰܗܰܪܢ: ܕ݁ܕ݂ܶܚܠܬܳܐ ܗ̱ܝ ܪܰܒܬ݁ܳܐ ܠܡܶܦܰܠ ܒܐܝܺܕ݂ܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܕܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܕܚܰܝ̈ܐ. ܘܐܳܦ ܫܠܝܺܚܶܐ ܛܘܽܒܳܢܶܐ ܐܰܟܪܶܙܘ: ܕܒܐܘܽܠܨܳܢܳܐ ܪܰܒ݁ܳܐ ܘܳܠܶܐ ܠܡܶܥܰܠ ܠܡܰܠܟܘܽܬܶܗ ܕ݁ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ. ܘܬܘܒ ܐܶܡܰܪ: ܕܰܕ݂ܚܺܠ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܝܰܘܡܶܗ ܕܡܳܪܝܳܐ: ܘܡܰܢܽܘ ܡܶܫܟ݁ܰܚ ܡܣܰܝܒܰܪ ܠܶܗ. ܐܰܙܺܝܙܳܐ ܘܚܰܡܝܺܡ ܪܘܽܓܙܶܗ: ܘܢܶܥܒܕܝܳܗ
And again he instructs us with these words: It is a fearful thing to fall into the hands of the living God. The blessed apostles also preached: Through much tribulation we must enter the kingdom of God. And again he says: The day of the Lord is terrible, and who shall be able to sustain it? His wrath is stubborn and fervent, and he shall make the earth a desert, and shall destroy the sinners out of it.


[p. 3] {#po.0.p3}


[p. 4] {#po.0.p4}
ܠܰܐܪܥܳܐ ܠܚܘܽܪܒܳܐ ܘܚܰܛܳܝ̈ܶܗ ܢܰܘܒܶܕ݂ ܡܶܢܳܗ܀ ܡܶܛܽܠ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܕܐܰܩܕܶܡ ܒܰܪ ܐ݈ܢܳܫܳܐ ܩܰܕ݂ܡܳܝܳܐ ܡܰܫܡܰܥܬܶܗ ܠܚܶܘܝܳܐ ܩܰܒܶܠ ܡܣܳܡ ܒ݁ܪܰܫܳܐ ܠܰܘܛܬܳܐ ܕܢܶܗܘܶܐ ܡܶܐܟܘܽܠܬܳܐ ܠܚܶܘܝܳܐ: ܘܰܐܪܡܺܝ ܠܰܘܛܬ݁ܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܟܽܠܗܘܽܢ ܝܰܠܕ݂ܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ. ܥܰܠ ܕܐܶܟܰܠ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܡܶܢ ܐܝܺܠܳܢܳܐ ܕܝܺܕ݂ܰܥܬ݁ܳܐ: ܐܶܬܟܠܺܝ ܡܶܢ ܦܺܐܪ̈ܰܝ ܐܝܺܠܳܢܳܐ ܕܚܰܝܶܐ ܕܠܳܐ ܢܶܐܟ݂ܘܽܠ ܡܶܢܶܗ ܘܢܺܚܶܐ ܠܥܳܠܰܡ. ܘܰܒ݁ܗܳܕܶܐ ܝܳܕ݂ܥܺܝܢܰܢ ܕ݁ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕ݁ܰܦܣܰܥ ܒܰܪ ܐ̱ܢܳܫܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܦܘܽܩܕ݂ܳܢܳܐ ܒܡܰܛܥܝܳܢܘܽܬܶܗ ܕܒܺܥܠܕ݂ܒܳܒܳܐ ܘܩܰܕ݁ܶܡ ܢܣܰܒ ܠܶܗ ܪܳܡܘܽܬ݁ܳܐ ܕ݁ܢܶܗܘܶܐ ܦܰܝܡܳܐ ܕܥܳܒܘܽܕܶܗ: ܘܰܒܦܘܽܪܫܳܢܳܐ ܕܝܺܕ݂ܰܥܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܩܰܒ݁ܶܠ ܗܘܳܐ ܣܝܳܓܳܐ ܒܰܝܢܰܘܗ̱ܝ ܠܐܝܺܠܳܢܳܐ ܕܚܰܝܶܐ ܕܰܒܥܶܠܰܬ ܢܟܝܺܠܘܽܬܶܗ ܕܒܺܥܠܕ݂ܒܳܒܳܐ ܐܶܬܟܠܺܝ ܡܶܢ ܦ݁ܺܐܪ̈ܰܘܗ̱ܝ. ܘܐܶܬܬܣܝܺܓ ܐܝܺܠܳܢܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܦܘܽܩܕ݁ܳܢܳܐ ܕ݁ܪܰܒ݂ܳܐ ܒܪܘܽܡܚܳܐ ܕܚܰܝܠܬܳܢܳܐ ܘܰܒܫܰܠܗܶܒܝܳܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܡܶܬܟܰܪܟܳܝܐ. ܘܐܳܦ݂ ܗܘܽ ܒܰܪܢܳܫܳܐ ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕ݂ܰܪܓ݁ܶܫ ܘܝܺܝܕ݂ܰܥ ܡܶܫܟ݁ܰܚ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܠܡܺܐܙܰܠ ܠܘܳܬ ܗܰܘ ܐܝܺܠܳܢܳܐ: ܐܶܠܳܐ ܡܚܺܝܠܘܽܬܶܗ
3. For because the first man gave ear to the serpent, he received the sentence of the curse, by which he would fall as prey to the serpent; and the curse descended upon all his descendants. Because he ate from the tree of Knowledge, he was prohibited from the fruits of the tree of Life, lest by eating from it, he should live forever. From these things, therefore, we know that when man had transgressed the commandment through the circumvention of the evil one, and had proudly presumed to become equal to his Creator, he obtained the discernment of knowledge, and a hedge was established between him and the tree of Life, the fruits of which he himself was defrauded of by the deceit of the evil one; and the tree, by the command of the Most High, was protected by a dreadful sword and a flame turning itself round about. And although man, having once experienced and been taught, knew the way to that tree, he was nevertheless powerless to cross the hedge because of his infirmity.


[p. 5] {#po.0.p5}


[p. 6] {#po.0.p6}


[p. 7] {#po.0.p7}
ܠܥܳܒܽܘܪܳܐ ܣܰܣܓܳܝܐ ܠܳܐ ܡܶܫܟ݁ܚܳܝܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ. ܡܶܛܠ ܗܳܢܳܝܐ ܠܳܐ ܗܘܽ ܗܳܢܶܐ ܐܝܺܠܳܢܳܐ ܕܚܰܫ̈ܶܝܐ ܒܒܰܣܣܺܝܡܘܽܬܶܗ ܠܳܐ ܐܶܬܟܠܝܼ ܡܶܢ ܣܢ̈ܬܩܶܐ ܕܢܶܐܟܠܘܽܢ ܡܶܢܶܗ ܘܢܺܚܘܽܢ: ܗܳܝܕ݁ܶܝܢ ܐܰܘܪܶܟ݂ ܣܰܝܘܟ݁ܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܘܣܰܪ̈ܥܶܦܬܳܝܬ݁ܶܗ ܚܕ݂ܳܐ ܘܐܰܘܦ݁ܶܛ ܫܰܒܘܽܩܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܠܥُܠ ܡܶܢ ܣܰܣܓܳܝܐ: ܘܰܚܕ݂ܳܐ ܦ݁ܺܐܪܶܗ ܒܛܰܝܒܘܽ ܠܒܰܪ ܡܶܢ ܬܚܘܽܡܳܝܐ ܕܰܟܪܝܺܟ݂ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܠܶܡܛܪܶܗ. ܘܰܒܢܰܝ̈ ܐ̱ܢܳܫܳܐ ܡܶܛܽܠ ܝܺܕ݂ܰܥܬ݁ܳܐ ܘܕ݂ܰܘܰܕ݂ܬܳܐ ܘܰܡܣܰܓܦܳܢܝܳܬܳܐ ܕܩܰܝܕܲܡܘ ܐܰܚܺܝܕ݂ܘ ܗ̱ܘܵܘ ܒܦܶܓܕܶܐ ܣܰܓܺܝܐܶܐ: ܒܗܳܢܳܐ ܦܘܽܪܣܳܚܐ ܕܐܳܣܚܘܽܬܳܐ ܐܶܬܦܫܰܝ ܡܶܢܗܘܽܢ ܡܘܽܠܟܳܢܳܐ ܕܠܰܘܛܬܳܐ. ܘܟܰܕ݂ ܐܰܪܓ݁ܶܫ ܒܥܶܠܕܒܳܒܳܐ ܒܗܶܐ ܩܰܠܚܠ ܒܬܰܪܥܳܬ݁ܶܗ ܘܰܒ݁ܺܬܛܶܠܝ ܕ݁ܶܢܥܳܬ݂ܶܗ ܘܰܪܓ݁ܶܙ ܥܰܠ ܦܺܐܪܳܐ ܘܐܳܟܘܺܠܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ. ܘܩܰܒ݁ܶܠܘ ܒܦܰܓܪ̈ܰܝܗܘܽܢ ܠܒܘܽܛܳܠܳܗ ܕܠܰܘܛܬܳܐ: ܘܰܙܟܳܬ ܚܶܟܡܬܳܐ ܕܰܫܪܳܪܳܐ ܠܰܢܟܝ̈ܠܘܽܬܶܗ ܕܒܝܳܫܳܐ. ܘܐܶܬܢܛܰܪܘ ܐܳܟܘܽܠܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܕܦܺܐܪܳܐ ܐܝܟ ܛܘܺܛܝܺܬܳܐ ܒܰܣܓܘܽܠܳܐ ܘܥܰܠ ܕܰܒܥܶܠܰܬ ܒܘܰܪܟ݂ܬ݁ܳܐ ܐܶܬܢܛܰܪ ܟ݁ܽܠܶܗ ܣܓܘܳܠܳܐ ܥܰܕ݂ ܢܶܫܠܰܡ ܙܰܒ݂ܢܳܝܐ ܕܣܳܡ ܠܥܶܠܳܝܐ. ܘܕܰܢܔܰܪ ܥܠܰܝܗܘܽܢ ܦܘܽܣܩܳܢܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܦ݁ܰܪܟ݁ܶܗ ܕܰܣܓܘܽܠܳܐ: ܘܠܳܐ ܨܒܰܘ ܕܰܢܬܘܽܒܘܽܢ ܒ݁ܚܰܝܠܳܗ ܕܒܘܽܪܟ݂ܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܢܶܒܫܠܘܽܢ ܘܢܶܚܠܘܽܢ
He was, however, not prohibited from eating the sweetness of that tree and living; for the tree extended its branches, and scattered its shoots, and spread its boughs across the hedge, and kindly dropped its fruits beyond the boundary by which it was fortified for its protection. Then, by the help of this remedy, the promised curse was averted from men, who previously, on account of the confused and fatal knowledge, had obtained many punishments. When the enemy noticed this, shame entered his mind, his arts were made void, and he was angered at the fruit and at those who ate it. These, however, received in their bodies the abrogation of the curse, and the knowledge of truth overcame the fallacy of the evil one. Those who ate the fruit were preserved like a grape in a cluster, and because of the blessing the whole cluster was preserved, until the time predetermined by the Most High was fulfilled. But, although the appointed time was extended for the rest of the cluster, they were nevertheless unwilling to be converted through the virtue of the blessing, so that they might mature, and mitigate the bitterness they had once received, and partake of the sweetness of the fruit, whose plant, cultivated by the wise husbandman, had long since been deprived of help
ܡܶܢ ܡܰܡܪܝܰܪܘܽܬܳܐ ܕ݁ܩܰܒ݁ܶܠܘ ܘܢܶܗܘܘܽܢ ܟ݁ܰܘ̈ܬܳܦ݂ܳܐ ܠܚܰܠܝܘܽܬܶܗ ܕܦܺܐܪܳܐ ܕܡܶܬ݂ܦܰܠܚܳܝܐ ܢܶܨܒܬ݁ܳܐ ܒܚܶܟܡܰܬ ܦܰܠܳܚܳܝܐ: ܕܙܰܒ݂ܢܳܝܐ ܣܰܓܺܝܐܳܐ ܐܶܬܓ݁ܰܠܙܰܬ݂ ܡܶܢ ܥܘܰܕ݂ܪܳܢܳܐ ܘܡܶܢ ܡܶܫܬܰܘܕܥܘܽܬܳܐ ܕ݁ܡܰܥܘܗܰܒܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܐܶܣܚܘܽܬܳܐ. ܘܰܒܢܶܡܦܬ݁ܰܚ ܬ݁ܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܒܶܥܠܰܬ ܫܠܳܡܳܝܐ ܥܪܰܩ ܥܰܡܛܳܢܳܝܐ ܕܪܶܠܝܳܢܳܐ ܕ݁ܣܰܓ݁̈ܺܝܐܶܐ: ܘܰܒܕ݁ܶܢܚܳܐ ܕܢܘܽܗܪܳܐ ܕܐܰܪܥܝܳܬܳܐ ܘܒܰܛܥܝܳܢܘܽܬ ܦܺܐܪ̈ܰܘܗ̱ܝ ܕܙܰܝܬܳܐ ܡܰܢܗܪܳܢܳܐ ܕܒܶܙܘ ܪܘܰܫܡܳܐ ܕܐ݈ܪܳܙܳܐ ܕܚܰܝܶܐ: ܕܒܿܗ ܡܶܬܓܰܡܪܝܺܢ ܡܫ̈ܬܚܳܫܶܚܐ ܘܟܽܘܡܪ̈ܶܐ ܘܡܰܡܠܶܟܶܐ ܘܰܢܒܺܝ̈ܶܐ: ܡܰܢܗܰܪ ܚܶܫ̈ܘܽܟܶܐ ܘܡܰܕ݂ܗܶܢ ܐܳܚܘܽܒ̈ܶܐ ܘܰܡܩܰܪܶܒ݂ ܠܬܰܟ̈ܳܒܶܐ ܒܐܸܪܳܙܶܗ ܛܡܺܝܪܳܐ: ܘܐܰܝܠܶܝܢ ܕܚܳܒܨܝܺܢ ܘܩܳܪܒ݂ܝܺܢ ܠܘܳܬܶܗ ܢܳܛܶܦ݂ ܛܰܠܡܶܗ ܒܐܶܕ݂ܢܰܝ̈ܗܘܽܢ ܟ݁ܰܣܺܝܩܳܬܳܐ: ܘܚܳܙܶܝܢ ܒܥܰܝܢܰܝܗܘܽܢ ܘܫܳܡܥܢܺܝܢ ܒܐܶܕ݂ܢܰܝ̈ܗܘܽܢ: ܘܨܺܝܪܝܺܢ ܕ݁ܶܡܘܳܬܶܗ ܒܕܶܓܫܰܝ ܗܶܡܣܰܝܫܗܘܽܢ: ܘܡܶܬܚܰܦܛܝܺܢ ܕܠܳܐ ܒܶܗܠܳܐ ܠܡܶܪܕ݁ܳܐ ܠܰܩܕܳܡܰܝܗܘܽܢ: ܘܡܶܫܬܰܘܛܚܺܢ ܠܡܺܐܙܰܠ ܘܛܳܥܶܝܢ ܕܒܶܣܬܰܪܗܘܽܢ: ܘܡܶܫܟܚܝܢ ܡܡܰܢܥܢܺܝܢ ܠܗܳܝ ܚܙܳܬ݂ܳܐ ܛܡܺܝܪܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܩܰܒܶܠܘ ܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܛܰܥܡܘܽܗ̱ܝ ܕܕܳܪ ܠܗܘܢ ܢܶܡܳܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܢܶܥܪܩܘܽܢ ܡܶܢ ܒܢܺܝ̈ܫܳܐ. ܘܗܳܝܕ݁ܶܝܢ ܗܳܘܶܝܢ ܗܶܝܡܚܺܝܪܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ݁ܺܝܩܶܐ ܒܥܳܠܡܳܝܐ ܗܳܢܳܝܐ ܕܢܶܬܝܺܗܶܒ ܒܗܘܽܢ ܐܰܬܪܳܐ ܠܰܬܝܺܝܒܘܽܬܳܐ: ܐܳܦ ܡܶܛܽܠܳܬܗܘܽܢ ܡܶܫܬܰܒ݂ܩܝܺܢ ܒܰܛܳܠܶܐ ܥܰܠ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܰܢܩܰܒܠܘܽܢ ܡܘܽܠܟܳܢܳܝܐ: ܕܬ݂ܶܕ݂ܢܰܚ ܫܶܡܫܳܐ ܕܛܰܝܒܘܽܬܳܐ ܥܠܰܝܗܘܽܢ. ܘܡܶܛܽܠ ܢܰܓܝܳܪܘܽܬ ܪܘܽܚܶܗ ܕܛܳܒܳܐ ܡܶܬܢܰܛܪܝܺܝܢ ܙܺܝܙܳܢܶܐ ܥܰܡ ܩܶܡܚܳܐ: ܕܠܳܐ ܐܢ ܢܳܨܒ݂ܶܐ ܡܳܪܶܐ ܙܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܟܰܕ݂ ܙܺܝܙܳܢܶܐ ܠܳܐ ܫܰܠܶܡܘ
and the knowledge of the given remedy. But the gate was opened for seeking peace, and darkness fled from the mind of many; the light of intellect shone, and the fruits of the splendid olive germinated, in which is the sign of the sacrament of life, by which Christians and priests and kings and prophets are perfected; it illuminates the darkness, anoints the infirm, and through its secret sacrament brings back the penitent. To those who hasten to approach it, it instills its sense into their internal ears; they see with their eyes, and hear with their ears; they contemplate its figures in the senses of their meditation, and strive to proceed forward without rest, advancing their step, and forgetting the things that are behind; and at length they arrive at the hidden vision, which they perceived when they tasted Him who fashions melodies for them, by which they may turn away from the evil one. Then they are made the leaven of the just of this world, through whom a place is given for repentance, and on whose account even sinners are left on the earth, so that they may receive promises, and be illuminated by the sun of grace. For by the long-suffering of the good [God], the tares are reserved with the wheat, lest perhaps, if the lord of the harvest


[p. 8] {#po.0.p8}


[p. 9] {#po.0.p9}


[p. 10] {#po.0.p10}


[p. 11] {#po.0.p11}
ܡܰܪܒܝܺܬܗܘܽܢ ܢܶܦܩܘܽܕ݂ ܕܢܶܬܥܰܩܪܘܽܢ ܘܰܢܚܰܝܶܢ ܚܶܛܳܐ ܐܳܦ ܚܶܛܳܐ ܟܰܕ݂ ܡܶܬ݁ܕ݁ܰܟ݁ܶܝܢ ܙܰܙܢܶܐ ܡܶܢܗܶܝܢ. ܐܶܠܳܐ ܡܶܛܪܳܐ ܕ݁ܰܡܪܰܒ݁ܺܝܐ ܠܙܰܪܥܳܐ ܛܳܒܳܝܐ ܗܽܘ ܡܰܡܫܰܚ ܠܙܰܪ̈ܥܳܢܶܐ ܙܰܪܥܶܗ ܕܒܺܝܫܳܝܐ. ܘܗܳܕ݂ܶܐ ܕܰܒܟܰܕ݂ ܛܳܒܳܐ ܒܝܰܕ݂ ܚܶܟ݂ܡܬܶܗ ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܐܶܒܛܰܠ ܐܘܰܡܳܢܘܽܬܳܐ ܕܰܒܢܰܘܽܗ̱ܝ ܕܒܺܝܫܳܝܐ ܕܣܳܝܡܺܝܢ ܬܪ̈ܶܝܢ ܥܶܩܳܪ̈ܺܝܢ ܕܛܳܒܬܳܐ ܘܰܕ݂ܒܺܝܫܬܳܐ. ܐܶܠܽܘ ܓܶܝܪ ܡܶܛܪܳܐ ܕ݁ܰܡܪܰܒ݁ܺܝܐ ܠܚܶܛܳܐ ܡܬܰܚܶܝܒ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܠܙܰܪ̈ܥܳܢܶܐ ܙܰܪܥܶܗ ܕܒܺܝܫܳܝܐ: ܝܰܕ݂ܝܺܥܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܬ ܕܰܬܪ̈ܶܝܢ ܟܝ̈ܳܢܶܐ ܡܶܫܬܰܡܫܺܝܢ ܒܰܒܪܝܺܬܳܐ. ܐܶܠܳܐ ܡܰܪܒܝܺܬܳܐ ܕܗܳܠܶܝܢ ܘܰܕ݂ܗܳܠܶܝܢ ܒܢܶܡܛܪܳܐ ܘܰܒܪܘܽܚܳܐ ܘܰܒܫܶܡܫܳܐ ܡܶܬܓܰܐܝܳܐ: ܘܡܰܥܣܶܩܪܳܢ ܒܶܪ̈ܝܳܬ݁ܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܚܰܕ݂ ܒܳܪܘܽܝܳܐ ܕܰܒܦܘܽܩܕ݁ܳܢܶܗ ܒܶܪ̈ܝܳܬܶܗ ܡܶܬܕܰܒܪܳܢ. ܣܰܓ̈ܺܝܐܶܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܰܚܺܝܬܰܝܗܘܽܢ ܛܳܒ̈ܶܐ ܒ݁ܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܒܺܝ̈ܫܶܐ: ܕܡܶܛܽܠܳܬܗܘܽܢ ܡܶܢܗܘܽܢ ܪܘܰܓ݂ܙܳܐ ܐܰܫܝܺܕ݂ܳܐ ܡܶܬܟܠܶܐ: ܘܒܰܙܒ݂ܰܢ ܕܡܶܬܬ݁ܪܰܥ ܣܝܳܓܳܐ ܕܥܰܠ ܒܺܝ̈ܫܶܐ ܘܠܰܝܬ ܡܶܢ ܛܳܒ̈ܶܐ ܕܩܳܐܶܡ ܪܘܰܓ݂ܙܶܗ ܕܥܶܠܳܝܐ: ܘܰܒܥܶܠܰܬ ܪܘܺܓ݁ܙܳܐ ܕܥܰܠ
should order the tares to be uprooted before they have grown, the wheat might also suffer damage while the tares were being purged. Indeed, the rain, which brings increase to the good seed, likewise makes the tares, the seed of the evil one, to germinate. And this the good [God] instituted in His wisdom, so that the artifice of the children of the evil one, who posit two roots, one good and one evil, might fall into nothingness. For if the rain that nourishes the wheat were to weaken the tares, the seed of the evil one, there would be no doubt that two natures had worked upon creation. But both [seeds] receive increase from rain, wind, and sun, and the creatures testify to one Creator, by whose command created things are governed. For there are more good people in the world than evil, and on account of them the outpouring of wrath is restrained from the latter; but when the hedge against the wicked is broken through, and not even among the just is anyone found who stands in the breach, the overflowing measure of sins excites the fury of the Most High, and because of the wrath [aroused] against the impious, the elect also are implicated in the zeal; and when the sword receives the command to strike, it destroys sinners and the just together, because the just did not stand quickly in the ruin of the hedge, just as
ܘܟܰܕ݂ ܣܰܦܣܺܝܪܳܐ ܠܶܡܚܪܰܒ ܡܶܬܦܰܩܕ݁ܳܐ ܡܰܘܒܶܕ݂ܳܐ ܒܺܝ̈ܫܶܐ ܘܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܥܰܠ ܕܠܳܐ ܐܶܣܬ݁ܰܪܗܰܒܘ ܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܠܰܡܩܳܡ ܒܬܘܽܪܥܬܶܗ ܕ݁ܰܣܝܳܓܳܐ ܐܰܝܟ ܕܐܶܡܰܪ ܢܒܺܝܐ ܟܰܕ݂ ܡܰܟ݁ܪܶܙ: ܕܰܒܠܶܝܬ ܒܥܰܡܝ ܓ݁ܰܒ݂ܪܳܐ ܕܣܳܟܰܪ ܣܝܳܓܳܐ ܘܩܳܐܶܡ܂ ܒܬܘܽܪܥܬ݁ܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܐܰܦ̈ܶܝܗ ܕܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܠܳܐ
the prophet declares, saying: I sought among my people for a man who would build up the hedge, and stand in the breach before the face of the earth, that I might not destroy it, and I found none.


[p. 12] {#po.0.p12}


[p. 13] {#po.0.p13}
ܐܶܚܰܒܠܝܗ: ܘܠܳܐ ܐܶܫܟܚܶܬ܀ ܘܐܰܝܟ ܕܩܰܕ݁ܶܡ ܘܚܰܘܝ ܐܶܬ݁ܳܐ ܗܳܕ݂ܶܐ ܒܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܕܳܪܶܐ ܩܰܕ݁̈ܡܳܝܶܐ. ܢܛܺܝܪܺܝܢ ܗ̱ܘܰܘ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܥܶܣܪܳܐ ܕܳܪ̈ܺܝܢ ܕܒܶܝܬ ܐܳܕ݁ܳܡ ܒܚܰܝܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܕܡܰܬܘܽܫܠܰܚ: ܘܒ݁ܶܝܗ ܒ݁ܙܰܒ݂ܢܳܐ ܕܡܺܝܬ ܗܘܳܐ ܛܰܘܦܳܢܳܐ ܘܣܳܦ ܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܘܐܶܬܚܰܒܰܠ ܡܶܢ ܪܘܰܓ݂ܙܳܐ. ܘܢܘܽܚ ܐܶܣܬ݁ܰܬܰܪ ܡܶܢ ܡܰܝ̈ܡܳܢܶܐ ܕܡܰܝܡܘܽܠܳܐ ܕܡܶܢܶܗ ܢܫܰܠܶܘ̇ ܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܐܚܪܳܢܳܐ. ܘܰܒܝܰܘ̈ܡܳܬܰܘܗ̱ܝ ܕܢܘܽܚ ܓܰܒܪܳܐ ܕܰܢܝܳܚܳܐ ܐܶܬܢܛܪܘ ܥܶܣܪܳܐ ܕܳܪ̈ܺܝܢ ܡܶܢ ܛܰܘܦܳܢܳܐ ܘܰܠܬܰܚܬ. ܘܟܰܕ݂ ܫܠܶܡ܂ ܙܰܒܢ݁ܳܐ ܘܡܺܝܬ ܫܰܢܺܝ ܐܰܒܪܳܗܳܡ ܡܶܢ ܐܽܘܪ ܕܟܰܠܺܕ݂ܳܝܶܐ: ܘܰܒܡܰܓ݁ܪܰܬ ܪܘܽܚܶܗ ܥܶܠܳܝܐ ܚܰܘܝܺ ܚܢܳܢܶܗ ܥܰܠ ܟܢܰܥܢܳܝܶܐ: ܕܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܟܠܰܬ ܚܰܘܒܰܝ̈ܗܘܽܢ ܠܳܐ ܫܶܠܡܰܬ ܠܳܐ ܨܒܳܐ ܠܡܰܘܒܳܕܘܽ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ: ܐܰܥܶܠ ܙܰܪܥܶܗ ܕܐܰܒܪܳܗܳܡ ܠܡܶܨܪܶܝܢ ܘܰܡܠܰܟ݂ ܫܘܽܥܒܳܕ݂ܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܒܪܶܗ ܚܰܒܺܝܒܶܗ ܕܢܶܫܠܡܘ̇ܢ ܚܰܝܘܒܰܝ̈ܗܘܽܢ ܕܐܳܡܘܽܪܳܝܶܐ ܘܰܕ݂ܢܶܬܚܰܙܝ̈ܳܢ ܬܶܕ݂ܡܪ̈ܳܬܶܗ ܒܰܡܫܰܥܒܶܕ݂ܳܢܰܝܗܘܽܢ. ܘܟܰܕ݂ ܐܰܦ݁ܺܝܩ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܒܚܰܝܠܶܗ ܪܰܒܳܐ
4. And just as he showed before, this happened to the just of the ancient generations. For ten generations of the house of Adam were preserved until the days of Methuselah; and at the time when he died, the flood occurred, and the world perished and was consumed by wrath. Noah, however, was rescued from the waters of the flood, so that the second world might be renewed by him. And in the days of Noah, men who were quiet, ten generations were guarded, from the flood and thereafter. And when, the time being fulfilled, he had died, Abraham migrated from Ur of the Chaldeans; and the Most High, showing Himself patient, displayed His mercy to the Canaanites; for when the measure of their sins was not yet complete, He did not wish to destroy them; He brought the seed of Abraham into Egypt, and imposed servitude upon His beloved son, so that the iniquities of the Amorites might be fulfilled, and that He might reveal His wonders to his oppressors. And when He led them out by His great power, He manifested His prodigies to them. He fed them in the desert with the food of His saints, and satisfied them with the bread of angels; in a place
ܒܡܰܕܒ݁ܪܳܐ ܐܰܪܘܺܝ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܡܰܒܘܽܥܳܐ. ܘܟܰܕ ܐܰܪܓܶܙܘ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܒܥܶܠܕ݁̈ܒܳܒܰܝܗܘܽܢ: ܘܣܰܝܒܰܪ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܠܰܚܝܡܳܝܐ ܕܡܰܠܰܐܟ݁̈ܶܐ: ܘܐܰܪܘܺܝ ܠܗܘܽܢ ܡܰܒܘܽܥܳܐ ܒܐܰܬܪܳܐ ܕܠܳܐ ܡܰܚܝܳܐ. ܘܟܰܕ ܘܰܒܪܰܡܘ ܪܘܽܚܶܗ ܘܐܰܪܓܶܙܘܽܗ̱ܝ ܩܳܡ ܡܘܽܫܶܐ ܒܬܘܽܪܰܥܝܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܥܰܡܶܗ ܘܐܳܦ ܒܚܰܝ̈ܘܗ̱ܝ ܕܐܰܗܪܘܽܢ ܟܳܗܢܳܐ ܪܰܒ݁ܳܐ ܡܚܰܣܝܳܢܳܐ ܘܣܳܝܡ ܥܶܛܪܳܐ ܒܪܘܽܓ݂ܙܳܐ: ܠܳܐ ܚܠܰܛܝܘ ܗ̱ܘܰܘ ܒܗܘܽܢ ܒܥܶܠܕ݁̈ܒܳܒܰܝܗܘܽܢ: ܘܰܒܝܰܘܡܰܘܗ̱ܝ ܕܰܒܗܘܽܪ ܛܘܪܳܐ ܐܶܬܓ݁ܰܪܝܰܬ ܒܗܘܽܢ ܟܢܰܥܢܳܝܳܐ ܡܰܠܟܳܝܐ ܕܓܳܕ݁ܳܪ ܕ݁ܰܥܡܰܪ ܒܬܰܝܡܢܳܐ.
without water He made a fountain flow for them, and when they exacerbated His spirit and provoked Him, Moses stood in the breaking of his people. Similarly, in the days of Aaron, the great priest, exercising propitiation and offering incense in the time of wrath, their enemies did not dominate them; but when he had died on Mount Hor, the Canaanite king of Gadar, who dwelt to the south, contended against them. And in the days of Moses, victory never failed them. But as he died on Mount Nebo, they were prohibited from the manna.


[p. 14] {#po.0.p14}
ܠܳܐ ܚܰܣܰܪ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ: ܘܰܒܝܰܘܡܰܘܬܶܗ ܕܰܒܛܘܽܪܳܐ ܕܢܳܒܘܽ ܐܶܬܦܰܣܩܰܬ ܡܰܢܳܐ ܡܶܢܗܘܿܢ. ܘܟܰܕ ܢܦܰܩ ܥܰܡܶܗ ܡܶܢ ܡܶܨܪܶܝܢ ܐܰܥܒܰܪ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܒ ܝܰܡܳܐ ܕܚܺܝܠܳܐ: ܘܟܰܕ ܛܒܰܥ ܦܶܪܥܘܽܢ ܐܳܠܘܽܨܳܐ ܢܶܣܒܰܬ ܡܰܪܝܰܡ ܦܠܰܓ݁ܬܳܐ ܒܐܺܕ݂ܳܗ ܘܫܰܒܚܰܬ ܠܓܰܐܝܳܐ ܡܛܰܒܥܳܢ ܗܘܽܢ: ܘܰܒܙܰܒܢܳܐ ܕܡܳܬܰܬ ܡܰܪܝܰܡ ܠܰܝܬ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܡܰܝ̈ܳܐ ܕܢܶܫܬ݁ܳܐ ܥܰܡܳܐ. ܘܰܒܫܰܝ̈ܘܡܰܫܽ ܚܶܙܩܺܝܐ ܡܰܠܟ݁ܳܐ ܐܶܬܦ݁ܰܨܝܰܘ ܡܶܢ ܬܪܶܝܢ ܡܰܠܟ݁ܶܐ ܐܳܬܘܪ̈ܳܝܐ: ܘܒܳܬܰܪ ܡܰܘܬܶܗ ܕܓܰܒܪܳܐ ܙܰܕ݂ܝܺܩܳܝܐ ܐܶܬ݁ܕ݂ܒ݁ܰܪܘ ܒܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܒܗܘܽܢ ܚܪ̈ܳܐ ܠܒܳܬ ܡܰܠܟܺܝܐ ܕܒܳܒܶܠ. ܘܝܘܽܫܺܝܳܐ ܡܰܠܟܳܐ ܙܰܕ݂ܝܺܩܳܝܐ ܕܰܟܝܿ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܐܺܝܣܪܳܝܶܠ ܡܶܢ ܛܰܡܐܘܽܬܳܐ: ܘܗܘ ܐܶܬܟܰܢܰܫ ܡܶܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܒܺܝܫܬܳܐ. ܘܒܳܬ݁ܰܪ ܡܰܘܬܶܗ ܣܓܺܝܘ ܐܳܠܘܽܨܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܕܐܝܺܣܪܳܝܶܠ: ܘܰܒܩܰܪܘ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܡܶܢ ܐܰܪܥܗܘܿܢ ܡܰܠ̈ܟ݁ܶܐ ܥܰܠܒ݂ܳܒܰܝ̈ܗܘ̇ܢ܀
God led the people who had gone out of Egypt through the dreadful sea, and with Pharaoh the persecutor immersed in the waves, Mary took a timbrel in her hand, and praised the Glorious One, who submerged them. But when Mary died, the people had no more water to drink. And in the days of King Hezekiah, they were liberated from the two kings of Assur; but after the death of the just man, his sons were led away as hostages to the king of Babylon. Josiah, a just king, purged the land of Israel from uncleanness, and died before the evil arrived; but when he died, the oppressors of Israel were multiplied, and they themselves were extirpated from their land by enemy kings.


[p. 15] {#po.0.p15}


[p. 16] {#po.0.p16}


[p. 17] {#po.0.p17}
ܠܳܐ ܐܶܫܬܰܡܥܰܬ ܨܠܘܽܬܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ݂ܝܺܩܶܐ. ܐܶܡܰܪ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܩܰܕ݂ܝܺܫܳܐ ܠܶܐܪܰܡܝܳܐ: ܕܰܒܗܳܢܳܐ ܙܰܒ݂ܢܳܐ ܐܶܢ ܢܩܘܽܡ ܡܘܽܫܶܐ ܘܰܫܡܘܰܐܶܝܠ ܩܕ݂ܳܡܰܝ: ܠܳܐ ܨܳܒܶܐ ܢܰܦܫܝ ܒܥܰܡܳܐ ܗܳܢܐ: ܐܶܠܳܐ ܐܰܦܰܩ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܡܶܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܐܰܦܰܝ̈݁ ܘܢܶܦܩܘܽܢ. ܘܐܶܢ ܢܺܐܡܘܽܢ ܠܳܟ ܠܰܐܝܟܳܐ ܢܶܦܘܽܩ܆ ܐܶܡܪ ܠܗܘܽܢ: ܠܚܰܪܒ݁ܳܐ ܘܰܠܫܶܒܝܳܐ ܘܰܠܟܰܦܢܳܐ ܘܰܠܡܰܘܬܳܢܳܐ: ܐܰܪܒܰܥ ܡܰܚܰܘ̈ܳܢ ܐܶܫܰܕ݂ܰܪ ܥܠܰܝܗܘܽܢ. ܘܐܳܦ ܠܚܶܙܩܺܐܶܝܠ ܐܶܡܰܪ ܠܶܗ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ: ܕܐܢ ܐܶܫܰܕ݁ܰܪ ܚܰܪܒܿܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܘܐܶܡܰܪ ܬܶܥܒܰܪ ܚܰܪܒ݁ܳܐ ܒܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܐܰܘ ܚܰܝܘܽܬܳܐ ܒܺܝܫܬܳܐ: ܘܢܘܽܚ ܘܐܝܰܘܽܒ ܘܕܳܢܺܝܐܶܝܠ ܐܝܺܬ ܒܳܗ ܒܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܗܳܝ ܚܰܝ ܐ̱ܢܳܐ ܐܳܡܰܪ ܡܳܪܶܐ ܡܳܪ̈ܰܘܳܬܳܐ: ܕܠܳܐ ܠܰܒ݂ܪܳܐ: ܘܠܳܐ ܠܒܰܪܬ݁ܳܐ ܢܦܰܨܘܽܢ: ܐܶܠܳܐ ܗܶܢܘܽܢ ܒܙܰܕ݂ܝܺܩܘܽܬܗܘܽܢ ܢܦܰܨܘܽܢ ܢܰܦܫܗܘܽܢ: ܘܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܬܶܗܘܶܐ ܠܰܚܪܳܒܳܐ. ܘܰܡܣܰܟܠܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܬ ܠܰܢ ܡܶܠܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܡܶܟܪܰܐ ܗ̱ܘ ܟܶܪܝܰܬ ܠܶܗ ܠܰܢܒܺܝܳܐ ܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܡܨܰܠܶܐ ܘܠܳܐ ܡܶܫܬܡܰܥ: ܡܶܛܽܠ ܗܳܕܶܐ ܢܦܰܫ ܠܶܗ ܡܳܪܶܗ ܪܘܽܚܝܐ ܒܬܰܚܘܝܺܬܳܐ ܕܗܳܠܶܝܢ ܬܠܳܬܳܐ ܙܰܕ݁ܺܝܩܺܝܢ: ܥܰܠ ܕܣܶܠܩܰܬ݀ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܡܰܚܫܰܒܬ݁ܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܠܶܒ݁ܶܝܗ: ܕ݁ܰܠܡܳܢܳܐ ܢܘܽܚ ܘܐܝܵܘܽܒ ܘܕܳܢܝܺܐܶܝܠ ܐܶܫܬܡܰܥܘ ܚܰܕ݂ ܚܰܕ݂ ܡܶܢܗܘܽܢ ܒܕ݂ܳܪܗ ܘܰܨܠܘܽܬܶܗ ܕܝܰܠܝ ܠܳܐ ܫܡܰܥ܆ ܢܘܚ ܐܶܫܬܡܰܥ ܘܐܶܫܬܰܘܙܰܒܘ ܒܢܰܝ̈ ܒܰܝܬ݁ܶܗ ܠܰܡܺܥܗ ܡܶܢ ܪܘܰܓ݂ܙܳܐ ܕܡܰܡܛܽܘܠܳܐ. ܘܐܝܽܘܒ ܨܰܠܝܺ ܥܰܠ ܪܰܚܡܰܘܗ̱ܝ
5. And when the measure of the impious superabounded, the prayer of the just was no longer heard. For the Most Holy One says to Jeremiah: At this time, if Moses and Samuel stood before me, my soul will not be delighted with this people; but cast them out from my face, and let them go forth. But if they say to you: Whither shall we go forth? say to them: To the sword, to captivity, to famine, and to death. I will send four scourges upon them. To Ezekiel also the Lord said: If I send the sword upon this land, and say: Let the sword pass through the land, or a most evil beast; even if Noah, Daniel, and Job were in this land, as I live, says the Lord of lords, they shall not deliver sons nor daughters, but they themselves shall deliver their souls by their justice; but the land shall be in desolation. It is easy, however, to understand that the prophet grieved greatly that he prayed and was not heard, and therefore that the Lord gave him an answer by the example of those three just men. For such a thought had entered his mind: Why were Noah, Job, and Daniel, each in his own generation, heard, and my prayer is not heard? Noah, indeed, was heard, and the children of his house were rescued with him from the wrath of the flood. Job prayed for his friends


[p. 18] {#po.0.p18}


[p. 19] {#po.0.p19}
ܘܰܥܒܰܕ݂ ܡܳܪܝܳܐ ܒܐܵܦܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܘܠܳܐ ܗܘܰܘ ܒܩܶܠܳܠܳܐ. ܘܰܠܕܳܢܝܺܐܶܝܠ ܐܶܬܓܠܝܰ ܐ̱ܪܳܙܳܐ ܒܰܨܠܘܽܬܶܗ: ܘܚܰܒܪ̈ܰܘܗ̱ܝ ܐܶܬܦܰܨܝܰܘ ܡܶܢ ܪܘܰܓ݂ܙܶܗ ܕܡܰܠܟܳܐ: ܘܐܳܦ݂ ܚܰܟ̈ܝܡܶܐ ܕܒܳܒܶܠ ܕܒܺܝ̈ܫܶܐ ܗ̱ܘܰܘ: ܐܶܫܬ݁ܰܘܙܰܒ݂ܘ ܘܶܐܬܚܰܝܰܘ ܒܰܨܠܘܽܬܶܗ ܕܕܳܢܝܺܐܶܝܠ. ܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܗܳܠܶܝܢ ܐܶܬܪܰܥܝܰ ܢܒܺܝܳܐ ܢܦܰܩ ܠܶܗ ܛܳܒ݁ܳܐ ܪܘܽܚܳܐ ܒܒܰܣܝܺܡܘܽܬܶܗ ܐܰܝܟ ܕܰܡܥܳܕ݂: ܕ݂ܗܳܠܶܝܢ ܬܠܳܬ݁ܳܐ ܓܰܒܪ̈ܝܺܢ ܙܰܕ݁ܺܝܩ̈ܺܝܢ ܕܰܠܚܰܕ݂ ܚܰܕ݂ ܡܶܢܗܘܽܢ ܒܕ݂ܳܪܶܗ ܫܡܰܥܶܬ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ: ܐܶܢ ܢܩܘܽܡܘܽܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡܰܝ ܒ݁ܗܳܢܳܐ ܙܰܒ݂ܢܳܐ ܠܳܐ ܒܰܠܚܘܽܕ݂ ܥܰܠ ܒܺܝ̈ܫܶܐ ܠܳܐ ܐܶܫܡܰܥ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ: ܐܶܠܳܐ ܐܳܦ݂ ܒܪܳܐ ܘܒ݁ܰܪܬܳܐ ܢܦܰܨܘܽܢ: ܐܶܠܳܐ ܗܳܢܘܽܢ
and the Lord granted his request, and they did not come to reproach. To Daniel praying, the sacrament was manifested; his companions were rescued from the fury of the king; even the wise men of Babylon, most evil men, were liberated and saved through Daniel's prayer. To these things, therefore, as the prophet considered them, the good [God] responded with His accustomed kindness: Those three just men, whom I heard individually in their generation, if they stand before me at that time, not only will I not hear them for the impious, but they will not deliver sons nor daughters; and they themselves shall deliver their souls in their justice; but the land shall be in desolation. The prophet, therefore, assented to the Lord's response.
ܒܙܰܕ݁ܺܝܩܘܽܬܗܘܽܢ ܢܦܰܨܘܽܢ ܢܰܦܫܗܘܢ: ܘܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܬ݁ܶܗܘܶܐ ܠܰܚܒܳܠܳܐ. ܗܳܝܕ݁ܶܝܢ ܐܶܬܛܦܝܺܣ ܢܒܺܝܳܐ ܒܡܰܦ݁ܰܩ ܒܪܘܽܚܳܐ ܕܡܳܪܶܗ܀ ܘܐܰܟ݂ܘܳܬ݂ܳܗ ܕܗܳܕܶܐ ܡܰܚܫܰܒܬܳܐ ܐܶܬܪܰܥܝܰ ܐܳܦ ܐܶܪܰܡܝܳܐ ܢܒܺܝܳܐ ܘܐܶܡܰܪ: ܕܕܡܶܛܽܠ ܡܳܢܳܐ ܡܘܽܫܶܐ ܘܰܫܡܘܽܐܶܝܠ ܐܶܫܬܡܰܥܘ ܚܰܕ݂ ܚܰܕ݂ ܡܶܢܗܘܽܢ ܒܕ݂ܳܪܶܗ: ܘܒܰܝܳܐܦܰܝ ܨܠܘܽܬܶܗ ܕܝܰܠܝ ܛܰܫܺܝ ܡܳܪܝܳܐ ܘܠܳܐ ܫܡܰܥ܆ ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܡܘܽܫܶܐ ܩܳܡ ܒܬܘܽܪܰܥܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܥܰܡܶܗ ܙܰܒܢ̈ܺܝܢ ܚܛܝܳܬܗܘܽܢ. ܘܐܳܦ݂ ܫܡܘܽܐܶܝܠ ܚܰܝܶܣ ܥܰܠ ܟ݁ܽܠܳܗ ܐܝܺܣܪܳܝܶܠ ܥܰܠ ܕܡܰܠܝܽܘ ܡܰܠܟܽܘܬܳܐ ܘܐܰܣܠܝܽܘ ܡܰܠܟܘܽܬܳܗ. ܘܬܘّܒ
6. Jeremiah the prophet, revolving a similar thought, had likewise inquired: Why were Moses and Samuel each heard in their own generation, but the Lord covered my prayer, so that He would not hear it? For Moses stood very often in the breaking of his people, and they, although they had sinned, were not destroyed; and Samuel obtained pardon for all Israel, after they, rejecting the rule of [God], had asked for a king. But the good and most clement God


[p. 20] {#po.0.p20}


[p. 21] {#po.0.p21}
ܐܰܦܝܺܣ ܠܶܐܪܰܡܝܳܐ ܢܒܺܝܳܐ ܕܐܶܢ ܢܩܘܽܡ ܡܘܽܫܶܐ ܘܰܫܡܘܺܐܺܝܠ
gave faith to Jeremiah the prophet, saying: If Samuel and Moses stood before me at that time, I will not hear them.
ܩܕ݂ܳܡܰܝ ܒ݁ܗܳܢܳܐ ܙܰܒ݂ܢܳܐ ܠܳܐ ܐܶܫܡܰܥ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ܀ ܘܐܳܦ ܒܙܰܒܢ ܗܘܢ ܗܳܠܶܝܢ ܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܓܰܢ̱ܒܳܪ̈ܰܝ ܚܰܝܠܳܐ ܒ݁ܰܙܒܰܢ ܙܒܰܢ ܐܶܫܬܡܰܥܘ ܟ݁ܰܕ ܡܨܰܠܶܝܢ: ܘܒܰܙܒ݂ܰܢ ܨܰܠܝܽܘ ܘܠܳܐ ܐܶܬܥܢܝܽܘ. ܡܘܽܫܶܐ ܢܒܺܝܳܐ ܪܰܒܳܐ ܕܟܽܠܳܗ ܐܝܺܣܪܳܝܶܠ ܕܒܰܨܠܘܽܬܶܗ ܡܩܰܒܰܠܬ݁ܳܐ ܐܶܬܦܰܨܚܰܘ ܥܰܡܶܗ ܡܶܢ ܪܘܰܓ݁ܙܳܐ ܙܰܒܢܺܝܢ ܣܰܓܔ̈ܺܝܳܐܢ: ܘܣܶܦܩܰܬ ܨܠܘܽܬܳܗ ܠܟ݁ܽܠܳܗ ܐܝܺܣܪܳܝܶܠ: ܘܰܒܙܰܒܢܳܐ ܐ݈ܚܪܳܢܳܐ ܟܕ ܕܠܳܐ ܣܶܦܩܰܬ ܨܠܘܽܬܶܗ ܠܰܩܢܘܽܡܳܐ ܕܢܰܦܫܶܗ. ܨܰܠܝܺ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܒ݁ܰܚܢܰܓ݁ܬ݁ܳܐ ܘܐܶܬܚܰܢܰܢ ܕܢܶܥܒܰܪ ܢܶܚܙܶܐ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܡܘܽܠܟܳܢܳܐ: ܘܐܶܡܰܪ ܠܶܗ ܡܳܪܶܗ: ܣܳܦܶܩ ܠܳܟ: ܠܳܐ ܬܰܘܣܶܦ ܬܘܒ ܠܰܡܡܰܠܳܠܘܽ ܩܕ݂ܳܡܰܝ ܦܶܬܓ݂ܺܡܳܐܝܳܐ ܗܳܢܳܐ ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܠܳܐ ܫܳܡܰܥܢܳܐ ܠܳܟ ܥܰܠ ܕܡܰܪܡܪܰܡܬܽܘܢ ܡܶܠܰܝ ܦܽܘܡܝ ܘܠܳܐ ܩܰܕܶܫܬܽܘܢܳܢܝ ܒ݁ܡܰܝ̈ܳܐ ܕܡܰܨܘܽܬܳܐ. ܐܘ̄ ܐ̱ܪܳܙܶܐ ܬܡܝ̈ܗܶܐ ܕܠܳܐ ܡܶܫܬܰܡܥܝܺܢ ܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐܽ ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܒܰܕ݂ܠܳܐ ܡܶܫܬܰܡܥܝܺܢ ܢܶܬܪܕܘܽܢ
7. But even those just men in their generations, and most valiant men, each in his own time, were sometimes heard while they prayed, and sometimes were not heard. Moses, the great prophet of all Israel, by whose most acceptable prayer the people were frequently liberated from wrath, when the supplication of one man was sufficient for all Israel, at another time prayed, but his prayer did not suffice for himself. For he supplicated groaning and implored that, passing over, he might see the promised land; but the Lord answered him: It is enough; do not repeat this word before me any longer; for I will not hear you, because you exacerbated the word of my mouth, nor did you sanctify me in the waters of contention. O wondrous mysteries, that the just are not heard! For when they are not heard, they are chastised before the judgment, so that they may not be punished in the day of vengeance. Thus Moses was chastised for his murmuring; his prayer was not heard, and he did not enter the promised land.
ܡܶܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܕܚܺܢܳܐ ܕܠܳܐ ܢܶܬܪܫܘܽܢ ܒܚܰܘܡܳܐ ܕܦܘܽܪܥܳܢܳܐ. ܒܗܳܕ݂ܶܐ ܐܬܪܕܝܺ ܡܘܫܐ ܥܰܠ ܕܡܰܪܡܰܪ: ܘܠܳܐ ܐܶܬܫܰܡܥܰܬ ܨܠܘܽܬܶܗ: ܘܠܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܡܘܽܠܟܳܢܳܐ ܥܰܠ. ܐܰܡܰܪܢܳܐܝܳܐ ܐܰܚܰܝ̈ ܒܬܘܰܟܠܳܢܳܝܳܐ ܫܰܪܺܝܪܳܐ ܕܰܒܟ݁ܽܠܙܒ݂ܰܢ ܡܶܢ ܒܪܺܝܫܺܝܬ ܘܰܠܥܳܠܰܡ ܫܟܚܝܢ
8. With true confidence, brothers, I assert that just and upright men are constantly, from the beginning


[p. 22] {#po.0.p22}


[p. 23] {#po.0.p23}
ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܙܰܕ݁ܺܝ̈ܩܶܐ ܘܟܺܐܢ̈ܶܐ ܥܰܠ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܐܰܝܟ ܕܰܟܬܝܺܒ. ܠܳܐ ܥܳܢܶܕ݂ ܙܰܕ݁ܺܝܩܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܩܕܳܡ ܥܰܝܢܰܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܡܳܪܝܳܐ: ܘܠܳܐ ܒܳܛܶܠ ܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܟܺܐܢ̈ܶܐ. ܥܰܠ ܗܳܕ݂ܶܐ ܣܳܗܶܕ݂ ܦܳܪܘܽܩܰܢ: ܠܳܐ ܐܶܬܝܺܬ ܕܐܶܩܪܶܐ ܠܙܰܕ݁̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܐܶܠܳܐ ܠܚܰܛܳܝ̈ܶܐ ܠܰܬܝܳܒܘܽܬܳܐ. ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕ݁ܨܳܒ݂ܶܐ ܕܢܶܙܕܰܕ݂ܩܘܽܢ ܚܰܛܳܝ̈ܶܐ ܐܶܬܳܐ ܡܫܝܺܚܳܐ: ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܠܰܝܬ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܠܗܘܽܢ ܡܶܕܶܡ ܣܘܽܢܩܳܢܳܐ ܠܰܚܠܝܺܡܶܐ ܥܰܠ ܐܳܣܳܐ: ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܠܝܬ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܠܗܘܽܢ ܟܪܝܺܗܘܽܬܳܐ. ܘܐܳܦܠܳܐ ܣܰܒ̈ܥܶܐ ܟܰܦܢܝܺܢ ܗ̱ܘܰܘ ܠܶܗ ܐܰܘ ܕܰܨܗܶܝܢ ܕܢܶܫܬܘܽܢ ܘܢܶܬܬܢܝܳܚܘܽܢ. ܗܘ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܗܳܟܰܢܳܐ ܩܪܳܐ: ܕܟܽܠ ܕ݁ܰܨܗܶܐ ܢܺܐܬ݁ܳܐ ܘܢܶܫܬܳܐ. ܘܰܢܒܺܝܐ ܩܪܳܐ: ܕܐܘ̈ܝ ܕܰܨܗܶܝܢ܆ ܙܶܠܘ ܠܡܰܝ̈ܳܐ ܘܣܰܒܘ ܠܟܘܢ ܕܠܳܐ ܟܶܣܦܳܐ ܘܰܕ݂ܠܳܐ ܕ݁ܰܡܝܳܐ ܚܰܠܒܳܐ ܘܚܰܡܪܳܐ: ܚܰܡܪܳܐ ܡܚܰܕ݂ܝܳܢܳܐ ܘܚܰܠܒܳܐ ܡܪܰܒܝܳܢܳܐ. ܘܰܦܘܽܠܘܣ ܐܶܡܰܪ ܥܰܠ ܗܳܕ݂ܶܐ: ܕܐܰܝܟ ܕ݁ܰܠܚܰܠܽܘ̈ܕ݂ܶܐ ܒܰܡܫܝܺܚܳܐ ܚܰܠܒܳܐ ܐܰܫܩܝܳܬܟܘܽܢ܀
and forever, to be found upon the earth, as it is written: The just shall not fail before the eyes of the Lord, nor shall the world be destitute of the upright. And the Savior Himself testifies to this: I came not to call the just, but sinners to repentance. For Christ came, desiring sinners to be justified; because the healthy did not need a physician, since they labored under no sickness; nor did those who were satiated hunger for Him, or desire to drink and be refreshed; for He Himself cried out: Everyone who thirsts, let him come to me and drink. And the prophet cried out: O you who thirst! come to the waters, and take for yourselves without silver and without price milk and wine, wine that gladdens and milk that nourishes, of which the Apostle says: As to little ones in Christ, I gave you milk to drink.
ܗܳܢܶܐ ܐ̱ܪܳܙܳܐ ܪܰܒ݁ܳܐ ܕܩܰܝܕܶܡ ܐܰܟ݁ܪܶܙ ܢܒܺܝܳܐ. ܐܶܡܰܪ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ: ܕܟ݂ܽܠ ܕܰܨܗܶܐ ܢܺܐܙܰܠ ܠܡ̈ܰܝܐ. ܘܰܡܡܰܠܝܳܢܳܐ ܕܰܢܒܺܝ̈ܶܐ ܐܶܡܰܪ: ܕܟ݂ܽܠ ܕܰܨܗܶܐ ܢܺܐܬ݂ܶܐ ܘܢܶܫܬܶܐ. ܢܒܺܝܳܐ ܫܰܕܰܪ ܠܡܰܝܒܘܽܥܳܐ: ܘܗܰܘ ܕܶܝܢ ܡܰܫܩܬܳܢܳܐ ܩܪܳܐ ܠܡܶܫܬܶܐ. ܘܐܰܟܪܶܙ ܢܒܺܝܳܐ: ܕܰܩܪܘܽܒܘ ܘܐܶܫܬܰܘ ܕܠܳܐ ܕܡܰܝܐ ܚܰܡܪܳܐ ܘܚܰܠܒܳܐ: ܡܶܢ ܗܰܘ ܕܐܳܪ̈ܓ݂ܳܢ ܥܰܝܢܰܘܽܗ̱ܝ ܡܶܢ ܚܰܡܪܳܐ ܘܚܶܝܘܳܪܳܢ ܫܶܢܰܘܽܗ̱ܝ ܡܶܢ ܚܰܠܒܳܐ:
9. But the prophet foretold this great sacrament. For he said: Everyone who thirsts, let him come to the waters. And He who fulfilled the prophets said: Everyone who thirsts, let him come and drink. The prophet sent them to the fountain; but He who provides the drink, called them to drink. The prophet cries out: Approach, and drink without price wine and milk, from Him whose eyes are redder than wine, and teeth whiter than milk;
ܘܠܳܐ ܐܰܝܟ ܢܒܺܝ̈ܶܐ ܕ݁ܰܓ݁ܳܠ̈ܶܐ ܕܢܳܟܬܝܺܢ ܒܫܶܢܰܝܗܘܽܢ ܘܡܰܟܪܙܝܺܢ ܫܠܳܡܳܐ: ܘܡܰܢ ܕܠܳܐ ܐܳܬܶܡ ܠܗܘܽܢ ܒܦܘܽܡܗܘܽܢ ܡܰܟܪܙܝܺܢ ܥܠܰܘܗ̱ܝ ܩܪܳܒܳܐ. ܘܠܳܐ ܬܘܒ ܡܶܬܕܰܡܶܝܢ ܒܟܿܳܗܢܶܝܐ ܥܰܘܳܠܶܐ ܘܰܢܒܺܝ̈ܶܐ ܕܫܘܽܩܪܳܐ ܕܡܰܠܦ݁ܝܺܢ ܒܝܰܓܪܳܐ ܘܩܳܨܡܝܢ ܒܟܶܣܦܳܐ: ܘܐܶܟܰܠܘ ܚܛܺܝܬܶܗ ܕ݁ܥܰܡܳܐ ܘܰܠܥܰܘܠܗܘܽܢ ܐܰܪܡܺܝܘ ܢܰܦܫܗܘܿܢ. ܘܰܗܘܳܐ ܠܶܗ ܟܺܗܢܳܐ ܐܰܝܟ ܥܰܡܳܝܐ: ܒܕܳܐ ܕܠܳܐ ܪܫܳܐ ܠܥܰܡܳܐ ܒܰܚܛܺܝܬܶܗ ܘܰܣܥܰܪ ܟܳܗܢܳܐ ܐܰܝܟ ܚܛܺܝܬܶܗ ܕܥܰܡܳܝܐ. ܘܟܰܕ݂ ܡܛܺܝܬ ܡܶܠܬ݁ܳܐ ܠܘܳܬ ܡܡܰܠܝܳܢܳܗ ܐܰܟܪܶܙ ܠܰܫܠܺܝܚܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ: ܕܡܰܔ݁ܳܢ ܢܣܰܒܬܘܽܢ ܕܡܰܔ݁ܳܢ ܐܰܟܪܙܺܝܬܘܽܢ ܣܒܰܪܬܶܗ ܕܰܡܫܺܝܚܳܐ. ܘܐܳܦ ܐܶܡܰܪ: ܕܨܳܒܶܐ ܐ̱ܢܳܐ ܕܰܕ݂ܠܳܐ ܢܦܰܩܬܳܐ ܐܶܬܠܶܝܗ ܠܰܣܒܰܪܬܳܐ܀
but not like the lying prophets, who bite with their teeth, and preach peace; and to him who does not give something into their mouth, they preach war against him. Do not also become like the iniquitous priests, or the prophets of lies, who taught for hire and divined for hire. They ate the sin of the people, and cast themselves down in their iniquity. And the priest was as the people, because he did not rebuke the people in their sin, and the priest imitated the sin of the people. But when the word came to Him who was to fulfill it, He preached to His apostles: Freely you have received, freely give. And the Doctor of the Gentiles, building upon that, says: I have preached the Gospel of Christ to you freely. And again he said: I wish to give the Gospel without cost.


[p. 24] {#po.0.p24}
ܘܰܒ݂ܗܳܕ݂ܶܐ ܕܟܶܬܒܶܬ ܕܰܒܟܿܽܠܙܒ݂ܰܢ ܢܟܺܝܬܺܝܢ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܘܟܺܐܢܶܐ ܥܰܠ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ: ܗܳܟܰܢܳܐ ܐܝܺܬ݁ܶܝܗ ܕܠܳܐ ܦܘܽܠܳܓ. ܡܶܛܽܠ ܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ
10. That I wrote that just and upright men will be found upon the earth perpetually is surely undoubted. For on account of the just, the good [God] makes His sun to rise upon the evil and upon the good. You send down your rain so that the impious may obtain sustenance. The just and the upright
ܛܳܒ̈ܶܐ: ܘܡܰܡܛܶܝܬ ܐܰܢ̱ܬ ܡܶܛܪܳܟ݂ ܕܢܶܬܦܰܪܢܣܘܽܢ ܥܰܘܳܠܶܐ. ܡܶܠܚܳܗ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܕܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܙܰܕ݁̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܘܟܺܐܢܶܐ ܐܰܝܟ ܕܐܶܡܰܪ ܦܳܪܘܺܩܰܢ ܠܰܫܠܺܝܚܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܛܘܽܒܳܢܶܐ: ܕܐܵܢ̱ܬܘܽܢ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܡܶܠܚܳܗ ܕܐܰܪܥܳܐ. ܕܐܶܢ ܡܰܠܚܳܐ ܬܶܦܟܰܗ: ܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܒܰܥܓ݂ܰܠ
are the salt of the earth, just as our Savior said to His blessed disciples: You are the salt of the earth. But if the salt were to lose its savor,


[p. 25] {#po.0.p25}


[p. 26] {#po.0.p26}


[p. 27] {#po.0.p27}
ܡܶܬܡܣܶܚܐ ܘܡܶܬܚܰܒܰܠ. ܒܰܕܡܘܳܬ ܢܰܦܫܳܐ ܠܦܰܓ݂ܪܳܐ ܢܳܛܪܺܝܢ ܠܳܗ ܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܠܰܐܪܥܳܐ: ܕܟ݂ܰܕ݂ ܬܫܰܢܶܐ ܢܰܦܫܳܐ ܢܳܦܶܠ ܦ݁ܰܓܪܳܐ ܘܡܶܬܚܰܒܰܠ ܡܶܢ ܩܝܳܡܶܗ. ܒܰܕ݂ܡܘܽܬ ܥܰܝܢܶܐ ܠܦܰܓ݁ܪܳܐ ܐܝܬܰܝܗܘܽܢ ܙܰܕ̈ܝ̈ܩܶܐ ܒܐܰܪܥܳܐ: ܕܐܶܢ ܬܶܣܬ݁ܡܶܐ ܥܰܝܢܳܐ ܦ݁ܰܓ݂ܪܳܐ ܐܶܫܬܰܓܢܝ ܡܶܢ ܨܘܽܪܬܶܗ: ܘܟܰܪܝܳܐܝܺܬ ܡܶܬܬܩܶܠ ܒܛܰܗܪܳܐ ܐܰܝܟ ܕܰܒܠܰܠܝܳܐ: ܘܠܳܐ ܬܳܪܶܨ ܠܡܶܡܪܕ݂ܳܐ ܒ̈ܗܰܠܟܳܬܶܗ. ܣܰܡܳܐ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܕܚܰܝܶܐ ܙܰܕ݁̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܒ݁ܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܗܳܢܐ: ܕܐܰܠܘܽ ܠܳܐ ܐܺܝܬܰܝܗܘܽܢ ܒܶܗ ܒܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܡܶܫܬܪܶܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܘܡܶܬܚܰܒܰܠ ܡܶܢ ܪܘܰܓܙܳܐ. ܪܳܘܰܚ ܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܟܰܕ ܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܒܓ݂ܰܘܶܗ ܒܰܕܡܘܳܬ ܦܰܪܕܰܝܣܳܐ ܒܡܰܒܘܽܥܳܐ ܕܡܰ̈ܝܐ. ܐܶܢ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܡܰܒܘܽܥܳܐ ܝܳܒܫܺܝܢ ܦܺܐܪ̈ܶܐ ܕܦܰܪܕܰܝܣܳܐ ܘܠܰܝܬ ܒܗܘܽܢ ܠܚܘܽܫܚܳܐ ܘܠܰܢܚܳܬܳܐ ܕܢܰܦܫܳܐ: ܘܡܶܬܥܰܩܪܳܢ ܐܳܦ ܓܘܽܦܢܰܝܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܘܚܳܪܩܺܝܢ ܒܢܘܽܪܳܐ: ܘܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܡܶܫܬ݁ܰܟܚܳܢ ܓܘܽܦܢ̈ܶܐ ܕ݁ܰܡܚܰܕ̈ܝܳܢ ܠܦܰܠܳܚܳܐ: ܒܰܡܘܽܬܪܳܢܳܐ ܡܶܬܟ݂ܰܠܝܳܢ ܘܰܡܚܰܘ̈ܝܳܢ ܦܺܐܪ̈ܶܐ. ܒܰܨܠܘܽܬܗܘܽܢ ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝ̈ܩܶܐ ܒܰܕܡܘܽܬ ܓܒܝܺܠܬܳܐ ܒܚܰܡܝܪܳܐ ܕܰܚܡܝܪܳܐ. ܡܕ݂ܰܒܪ̈ܳܢܰܘܗ̱ܝ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܕܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܗܳܢܐ ܐܰܝܟ ܕܡܶܬ݁ܕ݁ܰܒ݁ܪܳܐ ܐܶܠܦܳܐ ܒܥܶܝܢܳܬ ܡܰܠܚܰܝ̈ܗ. ܢܶܦܩܳܐ ܐܶܠܦܳܐ
the world would quickly waste away and be corrupted. As the soul the body, so the just sustain the earth; the soul departing, the body collapses and is dissolved from its state. As the eyes to the body, so the just in the earth; if the eye is blinded, the body loses its appearance and is vitiated, it begins to stumble at midday as if in the night, and does not direct its steps on the right path. The just are the medicine of life in this world; if, however, they did not exist in it, the world would be dissolved, and would be destroyed by wrath. The world containing the just exults like a paradise irrigated by fountains. If the fountain dries up, the fruits of the garden will be dried up, and will no longer be of use or for the refreshment of the soul; its branches also will be uprooted and burned with fire; but if vines are found that gladden the husbandman, they are cultivated with greater care, and provide fruit. The world is continuously preserved by the prayer of the just like a mass by the power of leaven. This world, indeed, is ruled by the just as a ship is governed by the skill of its sailors; the ship tossed by rushing waves is carried hither and thither by violent commotion; but cautious helmsmen stand and keep watch, so that they may lead the ship to


[p. 28] {#po.0.p28}


[p. 29] {#po.0.p29}
ܡܕ݂ܰܒܪ̈ܳܢܶܐ ܚܰܟ̈ܝ̈ܡܶܐ: ܘܰܡܡܰܢܥܺܝܢ ܠܳܗ ܠܶܐܠܦܳܐ ܠܰܐܬ݂ܪܳܐ ܕܰܢܝܳܚܳܐ. ܐܶܢ ܠܰܝܬ ܠܶܐܠܦܳܐ ܡܕܰܒܪ̈ܳܢܶܐ ܚܰܟ̈ܝ̈ܡܶܐ: ܠܳܐ ܡܶܫܟ݁ܚܳܐ ܪܳܕܝܳܐ ܠܡܺܐܢܳܐ ܘܛܳܒܥܳܐ ܐܶܠܦܳܐ ܘܐܳܒ݂ܕ݂ܳܐ ܬܶܐܓܘܽܪܬܳܗ. ܐܶܢ ܠܰܝܬ ܟܺܐܢ̈ܶܐ ܐܳܒܕܝܺܢ ܥܰܘܳܠܶܐ: ܘܐܶܢ ܠܰܝܬ ܙܰܕ݁̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܠܳܐ ܡܶܬܬܙܝܺܢܝܺܢ ܟܳܦܘܽܪܶܐ܀
the place of rest. If the ship lacks prudent helmsmen, it cannot arrive at the port, but is overwhelmed by the waves, and the cargo perishes. If the just were to fail, sinners would be taken away; and if the good were not found, the impious would no longer be sustained.
ܘܐܶܢ ܬܰܒܥܰܢܝ ܫܳܡܘܽܥܳܐ: ܕ݁ܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܐܺܝܬ ܕ݁ܺܝܩܶܐ ܘܟܺܐܢܶܐ ܥܰܠ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ: ܠܡܳܢܳܐ ܒܙܰܒ݂ܢܳܐ ܒܺܝܫܳܐ ܘܰܠܬܺܝܕܳܐ ܠܰܝܬ ܨܠܘܳܬܳܐ ܕܡܶܫܬܰܡܥܳܐ ܢܶܣܬܰܟܰܠ ܢܶܣܬ݁ܰܟܰܠ ܕܠܳܐ ܚܶܪܝܳܢ ܫܳܡܘܽܥܳܐ ܡܶܕܶܡ ܕܡܶܢ ܠܥܶܠ ܪܶܫܡܶܬ݂ ܠܶܗ: ܕܡܘܽܫܶܐ ܓ݁ܰܒܪܳܐ ܢܒܺܝܳܐ ܪܰܒ݁ܳܐ ܡܕ݂ܰܒܪܳܢܶܗ ܕܟܽܠܶܗ ܐܝܺܣܪܳܝܶܠ ܒ݁ܰܙܒܰܢ ܙܒ݂ܰܢ ܨܠܘܽܬܶܗ ܚܰܣܝܰܬ ܥܰܠ ܟ݁ܽܠܶܗ ܥܶܡܶܗ: ܘܰܒܙܰܒ݂ܢܳܐ ܐ̱ܚܪܺܢܳܐ ܐܳܦ ܠܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܐܰܦܰܝ̈ ܢܰܦ݂ܫܶܗ ܐܶܫܬܡܰܥ. ܘܐܶܣܬܰܟܰܠ ܕܪܰܒܿܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܗܳܕܶܐ ܥܰܠ ܝܶܫܘܽܥ ܦܳܪܘܽܩܳܐ ܪܰܒ݁ܳܐ ܘܰܒܪܶܗ ܕܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ: ܕܕܡܶܛܽܠ ܫܰܡܥܘܳܐ ܨܠܘܳܬܶܗ ܡܩܰܒܰܠܬ݁ܳܐ ܐܶܬܪܰܥܺܝ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܠܰܒ݂ܪܝܺܬ݁ܶܗ ܐܰܝܟ ܕܐܶܡܰܪ ܫܠܺܝܚܳܐ: ܕܗܘܽ ܪܰܥܺܝ ܠܰܢ ܥܰܡ ܐܰܒܘܽܗ̱ܝ. ܘܰܒܙܰܒ݂ܢܳܐ ܕܐܶܬܿܬ ܫܳܥܬ݁ܶܗ ܨܰܠܝܽ ܘܰܒܥܳܐ ܘܐܶܬܚܰܢܰܢ ܟܰܕ݂ ܠܳܐ ܣܢܝܩ. ܘܐܳܡܰܪ: ܐܳܒܝ ܐܶܢ ܡܶܫܟ݁ܚܳܐ ܢܶܥܒܪܰܢܝ ܟܳܣܳܝܐ ܗܳܢܐ. ܘܥܰܠ ܐܰܦܰܝ̈ ܢܰܦܫܶܗ ܨܠܘܽܬܶܗ ܠܳܐ ܐܶܫܬܰܡܥܰܬ. ܠܥܘܽܕ݂ܪܳܢܳܐ ܕܝܺܠܰܢ ܠܳܐ ܐܶܫܬ݁ܡܰܥ ܦܳܪܘܽܩܰܢ: ܕܘܳܠܶܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܠܶܗ ܕܰܢܡܘܺܬ ܕ݁ܢܶܬܶܠ ܠܰܢ ܪܰܗܒܘܽܢܳܐ ܕܡܺܝܬ ܐܰܟܘܳܬܰܢ
11. If, however, a hearer should ask me: Since there are just and good people upon the earth, why does it happen in a miserable and infested time that sometimes prayer is not heard? Let the hearer understand without strife those things which I was signifying above, namely, that Moses, a prophet and great leader of all Israel, sometimes obtained propitiation for the whole people by his prayer; but at other times he was not heard even for himself. But hear something more about Jesus, the supreme Savior, and Son of God, whose most acceptable prayer when God had heard, He reconciled Himself to His creature, just as the Apostle says: He reconciled us to His Father. But when His hour arrived, He prayed, He besought, and He entreated, although without any necessity, and said: My Father, if it is possible, let this cup pass from me; and this prayer by which He supplicated for Himself was not heard. But for


[p. 30] {#po.0.p30}


[p. 31] {#po.0.p31}
ܘܩܰܛܠܶܗ ܠܡܰܘܬܳܐ ܘܩܳܡ ܘܐܰܩܺܝܡܰܢ ܘܰܡܠܰܟ݂ ܠܰܢ ܕܢܺܚܶܝܐ. ܐܶܠܘܽ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܐܶܫܬܡܰܥ ܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܐܶܫܬ݁ܐܰܠ ܡܶܢ ܡܰܘܬܳܐ ܟܰܕ݂ ܦ݁ܰܓ݂ܪܳܐ ܠܒܺܝܫ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ: ܠܰܢ ܠܰܩܝܳܡܬܳܐ ܘܰܠܡܰܡܫܰܪ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܠܰܢ܆ ܠܰܒܶܒܰܢ ܒܡܰܘܬܶܗ ܘܚܰܕ݂ܝܰܢ ܒܰܩܝܳܡܬܶܗ. ܘܰܒܗܳܕ݂ܶܐ ܕܰܕ݂ܚܶܠ ܡܶܢ ܡܰܘܬܳܐ ܒܚܶܟܡܬ݁ܶܗ ܥܒ݂ܰܕ݂ ܠܘܳܬܰܢ ܕܢܶܬܥܰܕܰܪ: ܕ݁ܰܢܚܰܘܶܐ ܦܫܺܝܩܳܐܝܺܬ ܠܟܽܠܗܘܽܢ ܒܢܰܝ̈ ܐ̱ܢܳܫܳܝܐ ܟܪܝܳܗܘܽܬܶܗ ܕܦܰܓܪܳܐ ܕܰܠܒܶܫ ܐܰܟܘܳܬܰܢ. ܐܶܠܘܽ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܠܳܐ ܝܺܠܺܝܕ݂ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܐܰܟܘܳܬܰܢ. ܠܰܝܬ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܥܠܰܘܗ̱ܝ ܐܘܽܪܚܳܐ ܠܡܰܘܬܳܐ. ܘܐܶܠܘܽ ܠܰܝܬ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܒܰܢ ܡܶܢ ܪܘܺܚܶܗ ܕܚܰܝܶܐ: ܐܳܦܠܳܐ ܝܳܗܶܒ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܠܰܢ ܪܰܗܒܘܽܢܳܐ
our help the Savior was not heard; for it was necessary for Him to die, so that He might hand over the pledge to us, He who died just as we do; He killed death, He rose, and He raised us, and promised life to us. For if He had been heard when, clothed in a body, He prayed against death, who would have confirmed for us a certain resurrection? He strengthened us by His death, and gladdened us by His resurrection. But that He trembled at death, He did that in His wisdom for us, so that we might be helped, and so that He might openly show to all men the infirmity of the body, similar to ours, which He put on. For unless He had been born in the same way as we, He would not have had a way to death; and if there had been nothing in us of His spirit of life, He would not have given us the pledge of life, so that just as He Himself rises, we also might rise. Therefore, He died our death first, so that we might be strengthened by His death;
ܕܚܰܝܶܐ ܕܢܶܬܠܰܒܰܒ ܒܡܰܘܬܶܗ: ܘܩܳܡ ܒ݁ܚܰܝܠܳܐ ܪܰܒ݁ܳܐ ܕܠܳܐ ܟܪܝܺܗܘܳܬܳܐ ܟܰܕ݂ ܙܟܺܝܗ̱ܝ ܠܡܰܘܬܳܐ ܘܰܣܠܶܩ ܡܶܢ ܐܰܬܪܶܗ. ܘܚܳܕܶܝܢܰܢ ܕܡܶܬܒܠܰܥ ܡܰܘܬܰܢ ܒܚܰܝܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܘܩܰܒܶܠܢ ܪܰܗܒܘܽܢܳܐ ܕܚܰܝܶܐ ܒܡܰܘܬܶܗ. ܐܶܠܘܽ ܠܳܐ ܡܫܺܝܚܳܐ ܡܺܝܬ ܒܦܰܓ݁ܪܳܐ ܕܡܶܢܰܢ ܠܰܝܬ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܠܶܗ ܪܰܗܒܘܽܢܳܐ ܠܡܶܬܰܠ ܠܰܢ ܕܰܢܩܰܒܶܠ ܬܟܝܺܠܳܐܝܺܬ ܕܚܳܝܶܝܢܰܢ ܡܶܢ ܡܰܘܬܳܐ. ܐܶܠܳܐ ܟܽܠܗܘܽܢ ܙܰܕ݁ܝܶܩܘܽܬܶܐ ܒܢܰܦܫܶܗ ܓ݁ܡܰܪ ܐܶܢܽܘܢ: ܘܰܢܣܰܟܰܢ ܡܶܢܶܝܗ ܪܰܗܒܘܳܢܳܐ ܬܟܝܺܠܳܐܝܺܬ. ܕܚܳܝܶܝܢܰܢ ܡܶܢ ܡܰܘܬܳܐ ܠܳܐ ܟ݁ܰܪܝܳܐ ܠܰܢ ܒܝܰܠܕ݂ܳܐ ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܐܶܬܝܺܠܰܕ݂ ܐܰܟܘܳܬܰܢ: ܘܠܳܐ ܩܳܛܥܳܐ ܠܰܢ ܒܟܘܪ̈ܗܳܢܰܘܗ̱ܝ: ܕܐܳܦ݂ ܗܘ ܐܶܬܟ݁ܪܰܗ ܐܟܘܳܬܰܢ ܘܰܠܐܝܺ ܘܐܳܦ ܟܦܶܢ ܡܶܛܽܠ
then with great power, devoid of infirmity, He rose, when He overcame death, and ascended from the seats of this world. And we rejoice because our death was absorbed by His life, and by His death we received the pledge of life. If Christ had not died in the body assumed from us, He would not have had a pledge to hand over to us,


[p. 32] {#po.0.p32}


[p. 33] {#po.0.p33}
ܕܢܰܠܦܰܢ ܕܰܢܨܘܽܡ: ܘܐܙܕܟܝܺ ܡܶܢ ܫܶܢܬܳܐ: ܘܚܰܫ ܘܐܶܬܢܰܣܝܰ ܒ݁ܦ݁ܰܓ݂ܪܳܐ ܡܚܺܝܠܳܐ ܘܡܶܫܟ݁ܰܚ ܕܰܢܥܰܕܰܪ ܠܐܰܝܠܶܝܢ ܕܡܶܬܢܰܣܺܝܢ܀
which we would confidently receive, so that we might live through His death. He fulfilled all pledges in Himself, and we have confidently received the pledge from Him, so that we may live from His death. Nor let the generation sadden us, because He was born just as we are; nor let us be taken by weariness in sicknesses, because He Himself was affected just as we are by sickness, and weariness, and hunger, so that He might teach us fasting; He was overcome by sleep, He suffered and
ܐܶܬܝܰܠܰܦ ܘܐܶܣܬܰܟܠ ܘܐܰܦܩܺܝܣ ܪܶܥܝܳܢܳܟ݂ ܕܰܒܘܳܠܝܽܬ݁ܳܐ ܠܳܐ ܐܶܬܬܰܡܥܰܬ ܨܠܘܽܬܗܘܽܢ ܕܗܳܠܶܝܢ ܬܠܳܬ݂ ܢܒܺܝ̈ܫܶܐ ܪܰܘܪ̈ܒ݁ܶܐ ܘܙܥܽܘܪ̈ܳܬܳܐ ܫܰܦܺܝܪ̈ܶܐ ܕܥܰܠ ܐܰܦܰܝ̈ ܢܰܦܫܗܘ̇ܢ ܨܰܠܝܽܘ. ܠܳܐ ܓܶܝܪ ܟܺܐܢܳܐ ܕܰܕ݂ܝܰܬܺܩܺܐ ܐܶܠܳܐ ܐܶܢ ܡܳܝܬܳܐ ܡܰܢ ܕܟܰܬܒܳܝܗ. ܒܡܘܽܫܶܐ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܐܶܬܡܰܠܟ݁ܰܬ ܕܺܝܰܬܺܩܺܝܐ ܠܥܰܡܳܝܐ ܕܐܺܝܣܪܳܝܶܠ: ܕܢܶܬܶܠ ܠܗܘܢ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܰܟ݂ܢܰܥܢܳܝ̈ܶܐ ܠܫܘܽܪܬܳܢܳܐ. ܘܰܒܝܶܕ݂ ܝܶܫܘܽܥ ܐܶܬܡܰܠܟܰܬ ܕܰܕ݂ܝܰܬܺܩܺܐ: ܕ݁ܢܶܬܶܠ ܠܥܰܡ̈ܡܶܐ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܚܰܝ̈ܶܐ. ܡܺܝܬ ܡܘܽܫܶܐ ܒܰܥܒܳܪ ܝܽܘܪܕ݁ܢܳܢ ܘܩܳܡܰܬ ܕܺܝܰܬܺܩܺܝܐ ܕ݁ܰܡܠܰܟ ܠܥܰܡܶܗ: ܘܡܺܝܬ ܢܺܝܫܘܽܥ ܒܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܡܰܘܬܰܢ ܘܩܳܡܰܬ ܕܺܝܰܬ݁ܺܩܺܐ ܕܰܡܠܰܟ݂ ܠܥܰܡ̈ܡܶܐ: ܕܐܶܫܬܰܘܕܝܺ ܕܢܶܬܶܠ ܠܗܘܢ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܚܰܝ̈ܶܐ. ܠܡܘܽܫܶܐ ܚܰܘܝܶܗ ܡܳܪܶܗ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܡܘܽܠܟܳܢܳܝܬܳܐ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܥܕܰܟܧܠ ܕܳܐ ܚܰܝܪܬܳܗ ܐܺܝܣܪܳܝܶܠ: ܘܝܶܫܘܽܥ ܦܳܪܘܽܩܰܢ ܩܳܡ ܡܶܢ ܒܶܝܬ ܡܺܝ̈ܬܶܐ ܘܐܶܙܰܠ ܕܰܢܥܰܬܶܕ݂
was tempted in a weak body, and He can help those who are tempted. 12. Learn, therefore, understand, and have it persuaded in your mind that it was most fitting that those three most excellent prophets, and outstanding shepherds, while they prayed, were not heard, when they were supplicating for themselves. For the testament will not be firm unless he who wrote it has died. A testament was promised to the people of Israel through Moses, namely, that he would give the land of Canaan to them as an inheritance. Another testament was promised through Jesus, that He would give the land of life to the Gentiles. Moses died on this side of the Jordan, and the testament which he had promised to his people was confirmed. Then Jesus died in the land of our mortality, and the testament constituted by Him for the Gentiles was confirmed, promising that He would give them the land of life. The Lord showed Moses the land of promise, before Israel obtained it as an inheritance; but Jesus our Savior


[p. 34] {#po.0.p34}
ܠܰܢ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܐܶܫܬܰܘܕܝ̈. ܐܰܝܟ ܕܐܶܡܰܪ ܠܗܘܿܢ ܠܰܫܠܺܝܚܰܘܗ̱ܝ: ܕܐܰܝܟܳܐ ܐܳܙܶܠ ܐܶܢܳܐ ܕܐܶܛܰܝܶܒ ܠܟܘܽܢ: ܘܗܳܝܕܶܝܢ ܐܺܬ݂ܶܐ ܘܐܶܕܒܰܪܟܟܘܽܢ ܕܐܰܬܰܪ ܕܐܶܢܳܐ ܐܳܦ ܐܰܢ̱ܬܘܽܢ ܬܶܗܘܘܽܢ. ܘܐܰܫܰܪ ܠܰܢ ܡܘܽܠܟܳܢܳܝܐ ܕܥܰܡܶܗ ܢܶܗܘܶܐ. ܘܚܰܘܝ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܐܳܦ ܠܰܬܠܺܝܕ݂ܰܘܗ̱ܝ ܡܶܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܙܰܒܢܳܐ ܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܫܶܐܠܘܽܗ̱ܝ ܐܳܬܳܐ ܕܡܶܐܬ݂ܝܺܬ݂ܶܗ ܟܰܕ݂ ܕܒܰܪ ܠܰܬܠܳܬ݁ܳܐ ܬܰܠܡܺܝ̈ܕܰܘܗ̱ܝ ܠܫܶܡܥܘܽܢ ܘܰܠܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ ܘܰܠܝܘܽܚܰܢܳܢ: ܠܫܶܡܥܘܿܢ ܟܺܐܦܳܐ ܫܶܬܶܐܣܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܥܺܕ݂ܬܳܐ: ܘܰܠܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ݂ ܘܰܠܝܘܽܚܰܢܳܢ ܥܰܡܽܘܕ݂ܶܐ ܬܰܩܢ̈ܶܐ ܕ݂ܥܺܕ݂ܬܳܐ. ܘܚܰܘܝ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܠܗܳܠܶܝܢ ܬܠܳܬ݁ܳܐ ܣܳܗ̈ܕ݁ܺܝܢ ܫܰܪܺܝܪ̈ܶܐ ܐܬܳܐ ܕܡܶܐܬ݂ܝܺܬܶܗ ܟܰܕ݂ ܐܶܫܬ݁ܰܚܠܰܦ ܒܙܺܝܘܶܗ ܒܰܕ݂ܡܘܽܬ ܡܶܐܬܝܺܬܶܗ܇ ܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܡܘܽܫܶܐ ܘܐܶܠܺܝܳܐ ܠܘܳܬܶܗ. ܘܠܰܒܶܝܒ ܠܡܺܝ̈ܬܶܐ ܕܰܥܬܝܺܕ݂ܺܝܢ ܠܡܶܚܳܝܐ ܐܰܝܟ ܡܘܽܫܶܐ ܕܐܶܬܚܙܝ̈: ܘܡܶܬܬܰܟܠܝܺܢ ܚܰܝܶܐ ܕܡܶܫܬ݁ܰܚܪܝܺܢ ܠܘܳܬ ܐܳܬܺܝܬܶܗ ܕܡܶܬܚܰܛܦܝܢ ܠܐܘܽܪܥܶܗ ܒܰܚܕ݂ܡܝܘܬܳܐ ܐܶܠܺܝܫܳܥܐ ܕܠܳܐ ܛܥܰܡ ܡܰܘܬܳܐ ܘܐܶܬܚܙܝܺ ܠܘܳܬܶܗ. ܐܰܘܕ݁ܥܰܢ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܕܡܘܽܫܶܐ ܢܒܺܝܳܐ ܪܰܒܳܐ ܘܢܶܫܝܘܽܥ ܒܪܳܐ ܒܘܽܟܪܳܐ ܚܰܒܺܝܒܳܐ ܒ݁ܰܙܒ݁ܰܢ ܕܘܳܠܶܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܐܶܬܬܡܰܥܘ ܘܚܺܝܣܘ ܒܰܕܨܠܘܽܬܗܘܽܢ ܥܰܠ ܥܰܡܳܐ ܣܰܓܺܝܐܳܐ: ܘܒܰܙܒܰܢ ܐ݈ܚܪ̈ܺܝܢ ܐܶܬܦܠܚܘ
rose from the dead and went away, so that He might prepare for us the land promised by Him, just as He said to His apostles: I go to prepare a place for you, and then I will come, and I will bring you, so that where I am, you also may be. And He confirmed to us that promise that we would be with Him, [and this] He manifested before the time to His disciples inquiring about the sign of His coming, namely, when He took away His three disciples Simon, and James and John, Simon Peter, the foundation of the Church, and James and John, the most firm columns of the Church. To these three true witnesses, therefore, He showed the sign of His coming, when He was changed in appearance, as [it will be] in His coming, with Moses and Elijah standing by Him. He strengthened the dead, to whom it will happen that they shall revive, after the example of Moses who appeared; but the living who are left for His coming trust that they will fly upward to meet Him, just as Elijah, who did not taste death, and appeared with him. But to us [He] showed that Moses, the great prophet, and Jesus, the firstborn most beloved Son, were heard when it was necessary, and merited propitiation for many people through their prayer;


[p. 35] {#po.0.p35}


[p. 36] {#po.0.p36}
ܡܶܢ ܫܶܐܠܳܝܬܗܘܽܢ ܕܰܒܝܗܳܕܶܐ ܐܰܚܝܘܽܬܳܐ ܢܶܬܠܰܒܒܘܽܢ܂ ܣܰܓܝ̈ܺܐܶܐ ܐܰܝܠܶܝܢ ܕ݁ܰܡܨܰܠܶܝܢ ܘܠܐ ܡܶܫܬܡܥܝ̈ܢ: ܟ݁ܰܝܕ݂ ܢܶܬܬܢܝܢ ܒܬܰܚܝܘܽܬܳܐ ܕܗܳܠܶܝܢ ܓܰܙܒܳܪ̈ܶܐ ܚܰܝܠܳܢܶܐ ܕܡܨܰܠܶܝܢ ܘܠܳܐ ܡܶܫܬܰܡܥܺܝܢ ܠܳܐ ܟ݁ܰܪܝܳܐ ܠܗܘܽܢ: ܕܰܡܩܰܒܠܝܺܢ ܒܙܲܟ݂ܝܘܽܬܗܘܽܢ ܘܡܶܬܛܦܝܣܝܺܢ. ܡܶܢ ܕܡܘܽܬܳܐ ܕܡܘܽܫܶܐ
at other times, however, they were excluded from their petition, so that by that signification many might be excited, namely those who pray and are not heard, attending to the example of those most valiant men, who, when they were not heard in praying, did not take it ill; so that they may perceive in their mind, and be led to believe, by the example of Moses, and of our Savior, and of the prophets, and of the ancient just men.


[p. 37] {#po.0.p37}
ܘܰܕܦܳܪܘܺܩܰܢ ܘܕܰܢܒܺܝ̈ܶܐ ܘܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܩܰܕ݁̈ܡܳܝܐ܀ ܡܶܟ݂ܶܠ ܐܶܬܛܦܝܣ ܘܐܶܬܒܰܚܰܢ ܕܐܝܺܬ ܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܒܐܰܪܥܳܐ: ܘܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܡܶܛܽܠ ܟܺܐܢܶܐ
13. You, therefore, be persuaded and understand that the just exist on the earth, and that the world remains for the sake of the just. For the world subsists by goodness, just as the prophet says: The world shall be built by goodness.
ܩܳܐܶܡ ܐܰܟ ܕܐܳܡܰܪ ܢܒܺܝܳܐ: ܕܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܒܛܰܝܒ݁ܘܽܬܳܐ ܢܶܬܒܢܶܐ. ܘܗܳܕܳܐ ܗ̱ܝ ܛܰܝܒܘܽܬܳܐ ܡܚܰܬ݁ܰܪܬ݁ܳܐ ܕ݁ܰܥܕ݂ܰܡܳܐ ܠܥܳܠܰܡ ܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܠܳܐ ܥܳܢܕܝܺܢ ܡܶܢ ܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܘܠܳܐ ܡܓܰܪܕܺܝܢ. ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܐܶܠܘܽ ܓܰܪܕ݁ܝܽܘ ܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܡܶܢ ܥܳܠܡܳܐ: ܒܦܘܽܦܳܥ ܟܶܠܰܬ ܚܰܘ̈ܒܶܐ ܕܥܰܘܳܠܶܐ ܣܳܝܐܶܦ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܒܪܘܰܓ݁ܙܳܐ ܘܡܶܙܕ݂ܰܟܝܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܬ ܠܳܗ ܛܰܝܒܘܽܬܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܟܺܐܢܘܽܬܳܐ ܬܳܒܥܰܬ ܕܝܺܢܳܐ. ܘܡܶܛܽܠ ܗܳܢܳܐ ܟܺܐܢܶܐ ܫܒܝܩܝܺܢ ܒ݁ܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܒܥܳܠܰܡ ܦܘܽܩܪܳܐ ܕܡܶܛܽܠܳܬܗܘܽܢ ܠܳܐ ܬܶܙܟ݂ܶܐ ܟܺܐܢܝܘܽܬܳܐ ܘܐܶܥܘܽܠ ܒܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܕܩܳܐܶܡ ܒܛܰܝܒܘܽܬܳܐ ܠܶܡܬܒܰܥ ܕܝܺܢܳܐ ܒܐܰܬܰܪ ܕܠܳܐ ܕܝܺܢܳܐ. ܘܰܛܫܶܝܢ ܟܺܐܢܶܐ ܒܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܗܳܢܶܐ
The primary goodness is this: that the righteous do not depart from the world nor fail until the end of time. For if the righteous were to fail in the world, with the measure of the iniquity of the wicked overflowing, the world would be consumed in wrath, and justice would overcome goodness, demanding judgment. Therefore, the righteous are left on earth, in the age of falsehood, so that because of them justice may not prevail nor enter the world—which consists of goodness—to demand judgment where judgment is not present. The righteous are hidden in this world so that, by their


[p. 38] {#po.0.p38}
ܕ݁ܡܶܛܽܠܳܬܗܘܽܢ ܠܳܐ ܡܶܬܚܰܒܰܠ ܡܶܢ ܪܘܰܓ݁ܙܳܐ: ܒܰܕ݂ܡܘܽܬ ܛܘܰܛܚܳܬܳܐ ܕܰܛܡܚܺܪܳܐ ܒܰܣܓܘܽܠܳܐ܆ ܘܡܶܛܽܠ ܛܘܽܛܚܳܬ݁ܳܐ ܐܶܬܢܛܺܝܪ ܣܓܘܽܠܳܐ ܙܰܒ݁ܢܳܐ ܣܰܓܺܝܐܳܐ. ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܗܳܟ݁ܰܢܳܐ ܫܡܰܥܢܰܢ ܦܶܬ݂ܓܰܡܳܐ ܕ݁ܰܩܪܳܐ ܢܒܺܝܳܐ ܘܐܶܡܰܪ ܠܥܰܡܳܐ: ܕܐܰܝܟ ܕܡܶܫܬܰܟܚܳܐ ܛܘܰܛܚܳܬܳܐ ܒܰܣܓܘܽܠܳܐ ܘܐܳܡܰܪ ܓܰܒܪܳܐ ܠܚܰܒܪܶܗ: ܠܳܐ ܬܚܰܒܠܽܘܗ̱ܝ ܠܰܣܓܘܽܠܳܐ ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܐܝܺܬ ܒܶܗ: ܘܐܶܡܰܪ: ܗܳܟܰܢܳܐ ܐܰܥܒ݁ܶܕ݂ ܡܶܛܽܠ ܓܰܒ̈ܝܰܝ: ܘܠܳܐ ܐܰܝܫܶܠ ܠܟܽܠܗܘܽܢ: ܘܐܰܦ݁ܺܝܫ ܙܰܪܥ݁ܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ ܘܡܶܢ ܝܺܗܘܽܕ݂ܳܐ ܚܳܪܬܳܐ ܕܛܘܿܪܝ
grace, it may not be overturned by fury, like grapes hidden in a cluster. For indeed, because of a single grain, the cluster has been preserved for a long time. Thus, we have heard the word which the prophet proclaimed, saying to the people: 'Just as if a grain is found in a cluster, and a man says to his neighbor: Do not destroy the cluster, because there is a blessing in it'; he says: 'So will I do for the sake of my elect, and I will not destroy all, and I will bring forth a seed from Jacob, and from Judah an heir of my holy mountain.' The cluster is the people of Israel; the blessing preserved within it is Christ the King.


[p. 39] {#po.0.p39}
ܩܰܕ݁ܺܝܫܳܐ. ܣܓܘܽܠܳܐ ܥܰܡܳܐ ܕܐܺܝܣܪܳܝܶܠ: ܘܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܕ݁ܰܗܘܳܐ ܒܓܰܘܶܗ ܡܰܠܟܳܐ ܡܫܺܝܚܳܐ܀ ܡܶܢ ܪܺܫܺܝܬ݁ܳܐ ܗܳܕ݂ܶܐ ܛܘܰܛܚܳܬܳܐ ܢܛܺܝܪܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܬ ܒܐܳܕܳܡ ܒܝܘܽܟܪܳܐ ܩܰܕ݂ܡܳܝܳܐ: ܘܐܳܦܶܢ ܚܛܳܐ ܐܶܬܢܛܰܝܪ ܡܶܢܶܗ ܙܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ. ܘܐܶܬ݂ܢܰܛܪܰܬ ܒܘܽܪܟ݂ܬ݁ܳܐ ܒܫܶܬ ܘܰܒܟ݁ܽܠܗܘܽܢ ܕܳܪ̈ܰܘܗ̱ܝ. ܘܟܰܕ݂ ܚܰܒܶܠ ܟܽܠ ܒܣܰܪ ܐܘܽܪܚܶܗ ܥܰܠ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ: ܐܶܬ݂ܢܰܛܪܰܬ ܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܒܓ݂ܰܒ݂ܪܳܐ ܕܰܢܝܳܚܳܐ ܒܢܘܽܚ ܕܐܶܫܬܟܰܚ ܙܰܕ݁ܺܝܩ ܘܐܰܡܺܝܢ ܒ݁ܕ݂ܳܪܳܐ ܡܚܰܒܠܳܢܳܐ. ܘܐܶܬܢܰܛܪܰܬ ܒܘܽܪܟܬ݁ܳܐ ܒܩܺܝܒܘܽܬܳܐ ܕܩܰܝܣܳܐ: ܘܣܳܦܘ ܟܠܗܘܽܢ ܪܰܫܺܝܥܶܐ ܒܝܰܨܳܦܬܳܐ ܐܰܒܺܝܕ݂ܳܐ ܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܦܶܠܳܬܳܐ ܘܰܢܫܺܝܠܳܐ
14. From the beginning, the grain was guarded in Adam, the firstborn before all; and even after he sinned, the seed of the righteous was preserved from him, and that blessing was retained in Seth and in all his generations. But after all flesh had corrupted its way upon the earth, the blessing was preserved in the man of rest, Noah, who was found righteous and upright in a perverse generation. The blessing, therefore, was preserved in the wooden ark; but all the wicked perished in the outpouring of wrath, from whom the grain had been plucked and taken away. But when Noah and his sons went out of the ark, the blessing was left with Shem, the father of the righteous.
ܡܶܢܗܘܽܢ ܛܘܽܛܚܳܬܳܐ. ܘܰܢܦܰܩ ܢܘܽܚ ܘܰܒܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܡܶܢ ܩܺܝܒܘܽܬܳܐ: ܘܰܢܛܺܝܪܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܬ ܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܒܫܶܡ ܐܰܒܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ: ܦܶܪܩܰܬ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܪܘܽܚܳܐ ܒܦܘܽܡ ܓ݁ܰܒܪܳܐ ܕ݁ܰܢܝܳܚܳܐ ܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܡܒܰܪܶܟ݂ ܠܰܒܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܡܰܝܡܘܽܠܳܐ. ܐܶܡܰܪ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ: ܕܢܰܦܬܶܐ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܠܝܳܦܶܬ: ܘܢܶܫܪܶܐ ܒܡܰܫܟܢܰܘܗ̱ܝ ܕܫܶܡ. ܘܠܰܛ ܟܢܰܥܢ ܙܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܚܳܡ ܕܒܰܙܰܚ ܒܚܰܝܒܘܽܗ̱ܝ: ܘܐܶܬܓܠܶܙ ܡܶܢ ܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ: ܘܰܪܕܳܐ ܗܳܕ݂ܶܐ ܒܘܽܪܟܬ݁ܳܐ ܒܥܶܣܪܳܐ ܕܳܪ̈ܺܝܢ ܡܶܢ ܢܘܽܚ ܒܕ݂ܰܡܳܐ ܠܰܐܒܪܳܗܳܡ. ܚܰܝ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܛܰܘܦܺܝܢܳܐ ܬܠܳܬܡܳܐܐ ܘܚܰܡܫܺܝܢ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ: ܘܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ܘ ܘܰܣܓܺܝܘ ܒܢܰܘܽܗ̱ܝ ܘܰܒܢ̈ܳܬ݂ܶܗ ܠܰܐܪܥܳܐ ܕܟܰܠܕ݂ܳܝܶܐ: ܒܰܠܒܶܠ ܬܰܡܳܢ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܠܫܳܢܶܐ ܕܟ݂ܽܠܳܗ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ. ܘܢܘܽܚ ܩܰܘܺܝ ܒܐܘܽܪ ܕܟܰܠܕ݂ܳܝܶܐ ܟܰܕ݂ ܝܳܬܶܒܿ ܘܢܳܛܰܪ ܒܘܽܪܟ݂ܬܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܛܰܘܦܳܢܳܐ ܡܰܐܬܶܝܢ ܘܬܶܫܥܺܝܢ ܘܬܰܪ̈ܬ݁ܶܝܢ ܫܢܺܝܢ܀
For the Spirit declared this through the mouth of the man of rest, when he blessed his sons after the flood, saying: 'May God enlarge Japheth, and may he dwell in the tabernacle of Shem.' But he cursed Canaan, the son of Ham, who had mocked his father; this one was defrauded of the blessing, which descended through twenty generations from Noah to Abraham. For [Noah] lived three hundred and fifty years after the flood; and his sons and daughters were born and multiplied in his days. When they had arrived at Babylon, in the land of the Chaldeans, there the languages of the whole earth were confused. But Noah remained and dwelt in Ur of the Chaldeans, guarding the blessing of the righteous for two hundred and ninety-two years after the flood.


[p. 40] {#po.0.p40}


[p. 41] {#po.0.p41}
ܘܐܶܬܚܺܠܶܕ݂ ܐܰܒܪܳܗܳܡ ܒܐܘܿܪ ܕܟܰܠܺܕ݂ܳܝܶܐ ܒܰܫܢܰܬ ܬܡܳܢܶܡܳܐܐ ܘܐܶܫܥܝܢ ܘܐܰܪܬ݁ܶܝܢ ܠܚܰܝܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܕܢܘܽܚ: ܒܳܬܪ ܙܰܒܢܳܐ ܣܰܓܺܝܐ ܕܐܶܬܒܰܠܒܰܠܘ ܠܶܫ̈ܳܢܶܐ. ܘܟܰܕ݂ ܗܘܳܐ ܢܘܽܚ ܒܰܪ ܬ݁ܫܰܥܡܳܐܐ ܘܚܰܡܫܝܢ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ: ܫܠܶܡ ܙܰܒ݂ܢܶܗ ܘܡܺܝܬ ܒܣܰܝܒܘܽܬܳܐ ܛܳܝܒܬܳܐ ܒܰܦܢܰܬ ܚܰܡܫܝܢ ܘܰܬܡܳܢܶܐ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ ܠܚܰܝܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܕܐܰܒܪܳܗܳܡ܂ ܡܺܝܬ ܢܘܽܚ ܒܐܘܿܪ ܕܟܰܠܺܕܝ̈ܐ.
15. And Abraham was born in Ur of the Chaldeans, in the eight hundred and ninety-second year of Noah's life, a long time after the confusion of languages. And when Noah was nine hundred and fifty years old, he fulfilled his time and died in a good old age, in the fifty-eighth year of Abraham's life. Noah died in Ur of the Chaldeans. Then Abraham went out from Ur of the Chaldeans and traveled to Haran, and for a little
ܗܳܝܕ݁ܶܝܢ ܢܦܰܩ ܐܰܒܪܳܗܳܡ ܡܶܢ ܐܘܿܪ ܕܟܰܠܺܕ݂ܳܝܶܐ ܘܐܶܙܰܠ ܥܡܰܪ ܒܚܰܪܳܢ ܙܰܒ݂ܢܳܐ ܙܥܘܽܪܳܐ. ܘܡܶܢ ܚܰܪܳܢ ܫܰܢܝܶܗ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܠܰܐܪܥܳܐ ܕܰܟܢܰܥܢ ܝܰܠܕ݁ܶܗ ܕܚܳܡ ܙܰܪܥܳܐ ܠܺܝܛܳܐ ܕ݁ܰܡܠܺܝܟܳܐ ܥܠܰܝܗܘܽܢ ܥܰܒܝܕ݂ܘܽܬܳܐ. ܘܟܰܕ݂ ܗܘܳܐ ܐܰܒܪܳܗܳܡ ܒܰܪ ܡܳܐܐ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ ܒܰܫܢܰܬ ܐܰܪܒ݁ܰܥܡܳܐܐ ܘܬܶܫܥܝܢ ܘܰܬܪ̈ܬܶܝܢ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ ܠܚܰܝܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܕܫܝܼܡ ܐܰܒܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ: ܗܳܝܕ݁ܶܝܢ ܐܶܬܝܺܠܰܕ݂ ܐܝܺܣܚܩ. ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܫܝܼܡ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬ݁ܰܪ ܕܐܶܬܝܺܠܶܕ݂ ܐܝܺܣܚܳܩ ܡܳܐܐ ܘܐܰܬܪܰܥܣܪ̈ܶܐ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ. ܘܐܝܺܣܚܳܩ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂
time he dwelt there. And God moved him from Haran into the land of Canaan, of the son of Ham, the cursed race, to whom servitude was promised. And when Abraham was one hundred years old, in the four hundred and ninety-second year of the life of Shem, the father of the righteous, Isaac was born. And Shem lived one hundred and twelve years after Isaac was born. And Isaac, at sixty years of age, begat Jacob and Esau; and Shem lived sixty-two years after Jacob and Esau were born. And Abraham died when he was one hundred and seventy-five years old, and Shem lived thirty-seven years after the death of Abraham. And Isaac was one hundred and thirty-seven


[p. 42] {#po.0.p42}
ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܫܝܼܡ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܕܐܶܬ݂ܝܺܠܶܕ݂ܘ ܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ ܘܥܳܣܘܽ ܫܬܝܺܢ ܘܰܬܪ̈ܬܶܝܢ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ. ܘܡܺܝܬ ܐܰܒܪܳܗܳܡ ܒܰܪ ܡܳܐܐ ܘܫܰܒܥܝܢ ܘܚܰܡܫܝܢ
years old when he blessed his son Jacob, in whom the blessing of the righteous was hidden. And Jacob was seventy-seven years old when he fled to Haran, and eighty-four years old when he took Leah and Rachel as wives. And after one hundred and twelve years


[p. 43] {#po.0.p43}
ܫܢ̈ܝܢ. ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܫܝܼܡ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܡܰܘܬܶܗ ܕܐܰܒܪܳܗܳܡ ܬܠܳܬܝܺܢ ܘܰܫܒܰܥ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ. ܘܰܗܘܳܐ ܐܝܺܣܚܳܩ ܒܰܪ ܡܳܐܐ ܘܰܬܠܳܬܝܺܢ ܘܰܫܒܰܥ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ ܟܰܕ݂ ܒܰܪܶܟ ܠܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ ܒܪܶܗ ܕܒܶܗ ܛܡܝܳܪܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܒܘܽܪܟ݂ܬܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ. ܘܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ ܒܰܪ ܫܰܒܥܝܢ ܘܰܫܒܰܥ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ ܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܥܪܰܩ ܠܚܰܪܳܢ: ܘܒܰܪ ܬܡܳܢܝܢ ܘܰܐܪܒܰܥ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ ܢܣܒ ܠܠܶܐܝܳܐ ܘܰܠܪܳܚܶܝܠ. ܘܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܡܳܐܐ
of Isaac's life, Shem died, in the fifty-second year of Jacob's life. And when Jacob was ninety-seven years old, God said to him: 'Return to the land of your fathers, and I will do well to you, and I will be with you.' Therefore, when he
ܡܶܢ ܚܳܪܳܢ ܫܢܺܝܢ ܒܰܫܢܰܬ ܚܰܡܫܝܳܢ ܘܰܐܪܒܰܥ ܫܢܺܝܢ ܠܚܰܝܰܘܗ̱ܝ ܕܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ. ܘܟܰܕ݂ ܗܘܳܐ ܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ ܒܰܪ ܬ݁ܶܫܥܺܝܢ ܘܰܫܒܰܥ ܫܢ̈ܺܝܢ ܐܶܡܰܪ ܠܶܗ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ: ܕܰܦܘܽܩ ܡܶܢ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܰܐܒܳܗܳܬܳܟ ܘܐܰܛܶܐܒ ܠܳܟ ܘܐܶܗܘܶܐ ܥܰܡܳܟ. ܘܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ ܒܰܪ ܬ݁ܶܫܥܺܝܢ ܘܰܫܒܰܥ ܫܢ̈ܺܝܢ ܟܰܕ ܗܦܰܟ ܡܶܢ ܚܳܪܳܢ: ܘܐܶܬܚܰܣܰܠ ܐܰܒܘܽܗ̱ܝ ܒܰܪ ܡܳܐܐ ܘܚܰܡܫܝܺܢ ܘܰܫܒܰܥ ܫܢ̈ܺܝܢ. ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܐܝܺܣܚܳܩ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܕ݁ܰܗܦ݁ܰܟ ܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ ܡܶܢ ܚܳܪܳܢ ܥܶܣܪܝܺܢ ܘܰܬܠܳܬ ܫܢ̈ܺܝܢ: ܘܒܰܫܢܰܬ ܐܰܪܒܥܝܺܢ ܘܰܬܠܳܬ ܕܒܰܪܟܶܗ: ܘܥܶܣܪܝܺܢ ܘܰܬܠܳܬ݂ ܕܰܗܦܰܟ݂ ܡܶܢ ܚܳܪܳܢ: ܡܝܬ ܒܰܪ ܡܳܐܐ ܘܰܬܡܳܢܝܳܢ ܫܢ̈ܺܝܢ. ܘܰܢܚܶܬ ܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ ܠܡܶܨܪܶܝܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܥܶܣܪܝܺܢ ܫܢ̈ܺܝܢ ܕܡܝܬ ܐܝܺܣܚܳܩ ܐܰܒܘܽܗ̱ܝ: ܒܳܬ݂ܰܪ ܥܶܣܪܝܺܢ ܘܰܐܪܬ݁ܶܝܢ ܫܢ̈ܺܝܢ ܕܐܶܙܕܰܒܰܢ ܝܰܘܣܶܦ݂ ܒܪܶܗ ܠܡܶܨܪܶܝܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܫܬܝܺܢ ܘܰܬܡܳܢܶܐ ܫܢ̈ܺܝܢ ܠܡܰܘܬܶܗ ܕܫܶܝܡ. ܘܰܗܘܳܐ ܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ ܒܡܶܨܪܶܝܢ ܫܒܰܥܣܪ̈ܶܐ ܫܢ̈ܺܝܢ: ܘܡܝܬ ܒܰܪ ܡܳܐܐ ܘܰܐܪܒܥܺܝܢ ܘܰܫܒܰܥ ܫܢ̈ܺܝܢ: ܘܒܳܬܰܪ ܚܰܡܫܝܺܢ ܫܢܺܝܢ ܕܐܶܬܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܚܳܪܳܢ: ܒܳܬܰܪ ܥܶܣܪܝܺܢ ܘܰܫܒܰܥ ܫܢ̈ܺܝܢ ܕܡܝܬ ܐܝܺܣܚܳܩ ܐܰܒܘܽܗ̱ܝ. ܘܟ݁ܰܕ ܡܝܬ ܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ: ܝܰܘܣܶܦ݂ ܒܰܪ ܚܰܡܫܝܺܢ ܘܰܫܬ ܫܢܺܝܢ ܐܝܺܬܰܘܗ̱ܝ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ. ܘܐܶܬܢܰܛܪܰܬ ܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܒܝܺܗܘܽܕܳܐ ܚܰܠܕ̇ܗ ܕܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ ܥܰܕ݂ܠܳܐ ܢܶܥܘܽܠ ܠܡܶܨܪܶܝܢ. ܘܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܝܺܗܘܽܕܳܐ ܠܦܰܪܶܨ. ܘܗܝّ ܗܳܕܶܐ ܒܝܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܟܰܕ݂ ܢܚܶܬ ܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ ܘܝܺܗܘܽܕܳܐ ܠܡܶܨܪܶܝܢ ܒܚܰܨܶܗ ܕܦܰܪܶܨ ܛܡܝܪܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ. ܘܦ݁ܰܪܶܨ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܚܶܨܪܘܽܢ: ܘܚܶܨܪܘܽܢ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܳܐܪܳܡ: ܘܐܳܪܳܡ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܥܰܡܝܢܳܕܳܒ: ܘܥܰܡܝܢܳܕܳܒ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܢܰܚܫܘܽܢ ܪܰܒܳܐ ܕܰܒܢܰܝ ܝܺܗܘܽܕܳܐ܀
returned from Haran, he was ninety-seven years old; Isaac, his father, was one hundred and fifty-seven years old. And Isaac lived twenty-three years after Jacob returned from Haran; and in the forty-third year after he blessed him, and the twenty-third after he returned from Haran, he died, at the age of one hundred and eighty. Jacob went down into Egypt twenty years after the death of his father Isaac, twenty-two years after his son Joseph was sold into Egypt, and sixty-eight years after Shem died. And Jacob remained in Egypt for seventeen years and died at the age of one hundred and forty-seven, fifty years after he had returned from Haran, and twenty-seven years after the death of his father Isaac. When Jacob died, Joseph was fifty-six years old. The blessing, however, was preserved in Judah, the son of Jacob, before he entered Egypt. Judah indeed begat Perez; and this blessing, when Jacob and Judah went down into Egypt, was guarded in the loins of Perez. Perez begat Hezron, Hezron begat Ram, Ram begat Amminadab, Amminadab begat Nahshon, the father of the sons of Judah.


[p. 44] {#po.0.p44}


[p. 45] {#po.0.p45}


[p. 46] {#po.0.p46}
ܘܒܳܬܪܶܗ ܕܥܰܡܺܝܢܳܕܳܒ ܢܶܬܒܰܬ ܠܶܐܠܗܳܙܰܪ ܒܰܪ ܐܰܗܪܘܽܢ ܟܳܗܢܳܐ ܕܐܶܬܝܺܠܶܕ݂ ܡܶܢܶܗ ܦܺܝܢܚܳܣ. ܘܡܶܢ ܥܰܡܺܝܢܳܕܳܒ ܢܶܦܩܰܬ ܒܘܽܪܟܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܡܰܡܠܟܘܽܬܳܐ ܘܰܕܟܳܗܢܘܽܬܳܐ: ܡܕܰܒܪܳܢܘܽܬܳܐ ܘܡܰܡܠܟܘܽܬܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܢܰܚܫܘܽܢ: ܘܟܳܗܢܘܽܬܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܝܰܠܕ݂ܳܐ ܕ݁ܚܳܬ݂ܶܗ ܕ݁ܢܰܚܫܘܽܢ ܕܐܰܘܠܕ݂ܰܬ ܠܦܺܝܢܚܳܣ ܕܰܢܚܶܬ ܪܘܰܓ݁ܙܶܗ ܕܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܒܰܛܢܳܢܶܗ. ܘܡܶܢ ܢܰܚܫܘܽܢ ܐܶܬ݂ܝܺܠܶܕ݂ ܫܰܠܡܳܐ: ܘܫܰܠܡܳܐ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܒܳܥܳܙ: ܘܡܶܢ ܒܳܥܳܙ ܢܶܦܩܰܬ ܡܕܰܒܪܳܢܘܽܬܳܐ ܕܡܰܡܠܟܶܐ ܒܚܰܝܕ ܒܳܥܳܙ ܘܰܪܥܘܽܬ ܡܘܰܐܳܒ݂ܳܝܬܳܐ. ܘܡܶܛܽܠ ܗܳܢܳܐ ܢܣܰܒ ܒܳܥܳܙ ܠܰܪܥܘܽܬ ܡܘܰܐܳܒ݂ܳܝܬܳܐ ܕܢܶܗܘܶܐ ܠܘܽܛ ܫܰܘܬܳܦܳܐ ܒܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܐ: ܘܡܶܢ ܝܰܠܕ݂ܳܗ ܕܰܪܥܘܽܬ ܐܶܬܝܰܠܕ݁ܰܬ ܫܰܪܒ݁ܬܳܐ ܕܒܶܝܬ ܕ݁ܰܘܝܺܕ݂: ܘܡܶܢ ܙܰܪܥܗܘܽܢ ܐܶܬܝܺܠܕ݂ ܡܰܠܟ݁ܳܐ ܡܫܺܝܚܳܝܐ. ܘܠܳܐ ܛܠܰܡ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܠܰܐܟ݂ܣܢܳܝܘܽܬܶܗ ܕܠܘّܛ ܒܰܪ ܐܰܚܘܽܗ̱ܝ ܕܐܰܒܪܳܗܳܡ܂ ܕ݁ܰܢܦܰܩ ܥܰܡܶܝܗ ܡܶܢ ܚܰܪܳܢ: ܘܒܰܝܕ݂ܩܰܢܶܝܗ ܐܰܟܣܺܢܳܝܶܐ ܐܰܝܟ ܐܰܒܪܳܗܳܡ ܕܳܕ݂ܶܗ ܫܰܘܬܦܶܗ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܒܝܒܘܽܪܟ݁ܬܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܒܚܰܝܕ݂ ܪܥܘܽܬ ܡܘܰܐܳܒ݂ܳܝܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܢܰܣܒܳܝܗ ܒܳܥܳܙ ܐܰܢ̱ܬܬܳܐ. ܘܐܰܦ ܐܶܬܕܰܟܪܶܗ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܠܠܘܽܛ ܒܚܰܝܕ ܢܰܥܡܳܐ ܥܰܡܘܽܢܳܝܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܰܢܣܰܒ ܫܠܶܝܡܘܽܢ ܘܢܶܠܕ݂ܰܬ
16. And after Amminadab, [the blessing] rested upon Eleazar, the son of Aaron, the priest, from whom Phinehas was born. From Amminadab proceeded the blessing of kingship and priesthood, namely, the empire and kingdom from Nahshon, but the priesthood from the offspring of Nahshon's sister, who bore Phinehas, he who restrained the wrath of God by his zeal. From Nahshon was born Salma, and Salma begat Boaz. From Boaz, however, came the dominion of kings, through Boaz, indeed, and Ruth the Moabitess. For Boaz took Ruth the Moabitess as his wife so that Lot might become a participant in the blessing of the righteous. And from the progeny of Ruth arose the family of the house of David, and from their seed was born Christ the King. Therefore, God did not despise Lot, the son of Abraham's brother, as a stranger, who had gone out with him from Haran; and because he had received strangers after the example of his uncle Abraham, he received from God through Ruth the Moabitess, whom Boaz had taken as his wife, a part in the blessing of the righteous. Again, the Lord also remembered Lot through Naamah, the Ammonitess, whom Solomon had married, and who bore King Rehoboam. Solomon indeed took many wives, seven hundred noblewomen and three hundred concubines. But it is not written that he had seed from a thousand wives, except
ܢܣܰܒ ܫܠܺܝܡܘܽܢ: ܫܒܰܥܡܳܐܐ ܚܺܐܪܳܬܳܐ ܘܰܬܠܳܬܡܳܐܐ ܕ̈ܘܽܟܳܬܳܐ: ܘܠܳܐ ܟܬܝّܒ ܕܡܶܢ ܐܰܠܶܦ݂ ܢܶܫܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܘܰܐܝܬ ܠܶܗ ܙܰܪܥܳܐ ܡܶܢܗܶܝܢ ܐܶܠܳܐ ܪܚܰܒܥܰܡ ܡܶܢ ܢܰܥܡܳܐ ܥܰܡܘܽܢܳܝܬܳܐ ܕ݁ܡܶܢ ܚܰܝܰܘ̈ܗܝ ܕ݁ܰܘܝܺܕ݂ ܐܰܒܘܽܗ̱ܝ ܘܰܐܝܬ ܠܶܗ ܐܰܢ̱ܬ݁ܬܳܐ: ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܟܰܕ݂ ܩܳܐܡ ܫܠܶܝܡܘܽܢ ܒܡܰܠܟ݁ܘܽܬܳܐ ܪܚܰܒܥܰܡ ܒܰܪ ܫܰܢܬܳܐ ܚܕܳܐ ܐܲܝܺܬܰܘܗ̱ܝ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ. ܐܰܡܠܶܟ݂ ܫܠܶܝܡܘܽܢ ܐܰܪܒܥܝܢ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ: ܘܟܕ ܡܳܝܬ ܫܠܺܝܡܘܽܢ ܐܰܡܠܶܟ݂ ܪܚܰܒܥܰܡ
Rehoboam from Naamah the Ammonitess, whom he had taken while his father David was still alive; for when Solomon obtained the kingdom, Rehoboam was one year old. Solomon reigned forty years, and upon his death, Rehoboam reigned, a man of forty-one years.


[p. 47] {#po.0.p47}


[p. 48] {#po.0.p48}


[p. 49] {#po.0.p49}
ܘܰܗܘܳܐ ܠܘܽܛ ܒܝܰܕ ܪܘܽܬ ܡܘܽܐܳܒ݂ܳܝܬ݁ܳܐ ܘܐܶܫܬܰܘܙܰܦ݂ ܠܘܽܛ ܒܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܒܚܰܕ݂ ܪܥܘܳܐ ܡܘܽܐܳܒ݂ܳܝܬ݁ܳܐ ܘܢܰܥܡܳܐ ܥܰܡܘܽܢܳܝܬܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܬܪܶܝܢ ܫܰܪ̈ܒܳܬܳܐ ܕܰܗܘܰܘ ܡܢܶܗ. ܘܒܳܥܳܙ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܥܘܽܒܶܕ݂ ܘܥܘܽܒܶܕ݂ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܐܝܺܫܰܝ ܘܐܝܺܫܰܝ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܕ݂ܰܘܝܺܕ݂. ܘܐܶܬܢܰܛܪܰܬ ܗܳܕܶܐ ܒܘܽܪܟܬ݁ܳܐ ܒܟܽܠܗܘܽܢ ܡܰܠ̈ܟ݁ܶܐ ܕܒܶܝܬ ܝܺܗܘܽܕܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܕ݁ܰܘܝܺܕ݂ ܥܕ݂ܰܡܳܐ ܠܝܘܽܟܰܢܝܳܐ ܒܡܰܠ̈ܟ݁ܶܐ ܬܡܳܢܬܰܥܣܰܪ. ܚܛܳܝܐ ܕ݁ܰܘܝܺܕ݂ ܘܰܒܒܘܽܪܟ݁ܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܰܛܡܺܝܪܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܒܶܝܗ ܐܶܬܚܰܣܝܺ ܡܶܢ ܥܰܘܠܶܗ. ܘܰܥܒܰܪ ܫܠܶܝܡܘܿܢ ܢܳܡܘܽܣܳܐ ܘܰܫܒܰܩ ܠܰܐܠܳܗܶܗ: ܘܡܶܛܽܠ ܒܘܽܪܟܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܰܛܡܺܝܪܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܬ ܒܶܝܗ ܘܰܒ݂ܙܰܪܥܶܗ ܐܶܡܰܪ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ: ܕ݂ܪܰܒ݁ܳܐ ܐܰܥܒܕ݂ܝܺܝܘܗ̱ܝ ܟܽܠܗܘܽܢ ܝܰܘ̈ܡܳܬܶܗ. ܘܐܰܣܓ݁ܺܝ ܣܰܟܠܘܽܬܳܐ ܪܚܰܒܥܰܡ: ܘܡܶܛܽܠ ܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܕܒܶܗ: ܐܶܡܰܪ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ: ܕܠܳܐ ܐܶܦܨܶܐ ܟ݁ܽܠܳܝܗ ܡܰܠܟܘܽܬܳܐ ܡܶܢܶܗ: ܐܶܠܳܐ ܐܶܬܶܠ ܠܶܝܗ ܫܪܳܓ݁ܳܐ ܒܐܘܽܪܶܫܠܶܡ
17. Therefore, Lot participated in the blessing of the righteous through Ruth the Moabitess and Naamah the Ammonitess, from the two tribes originating from him. Boaz begat Obed, Obed begat Jesse, Jesse begat David, and this blessing was preserved in all the kings of the house of Judah, from David to Jeconiah, nineteen kings. David indeed sinned, but because of the blessing hidden within him, he was cleansed from sin. Solomon violated the law and abandoned his God; but because of the blessing to be preserved in him and in his seed, God said: 'I will appoint him leader all his days.' Rehoboam also increased sin, but because of the blessing that was in him, God said: 'I will not tear away the whole kingdom from him, but I will give him a lamp in Jerusalem, for the sake of David my servant.' Abijah, the son of Rehoboam, also sinned, but because of the blessing that was in him, Jeroboam was delivered into his hands
ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕ݁ܰܘܝܺܕ݂ ܥܰܒܕܝ. ܘܰܚܛܳܝܐ ܐܰܒܺܝܳܐ ܒܪܶܗ ܕܰܪܚܰܒܥܰܡ: ܘܡܶܛܽܠ ܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܕ݁ܒ݂ܶܝܗ ܐܶܬܝܰܒܠܶܬ ܒܐܝܺܕܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܝܘܽܪܳܒܥܰܡ ܘܚܰܝܠܳܝܗ. ܘܰܒܚܙܳܐ: ܪܶܬܚܰܬ ܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܘܰܦܠܰܚ ܙܰܕ݂ܝܺܩܝܘܽܬܳܐ ܘܰܫܡܰܥ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܨܠܘܽܬܶܗ ܘܐܶܫܬܠܶܡܘ ܒܝܰܕܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܒܥܶܠܕܒܳܒܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ. ܘܐܶܬܢܰܛܪܰܬ ܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܒܝܘܽܫܳܦܳܛ ܘܰܥܒܰܕ݂ ܕܫܰܦܺܝܪ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܡܳܪܝܳܐ. ܘܐܰܣܓ݁ܺܝ ܠܡܶܚܛܳܐ ܝܳܗܘܽܪܳܡ: ܘܡܶܛܽܠ ܒܘܽܪܟ݁ܬ݁ܳܐ ܐܶܬܢܛܰܪ: ܘܰܚܛܳܝܐ ܘܠܳܐ ܐܶܬܥܩܰܪ ܐܰܟ ܒܶܝܬܶܗ ܕܐܳܚܳܒ: ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܚܰܬܢܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܕܒܶܝܬ ܐܳܚܳܒ: ܘܐܰܛܥܝܰܬܶܗ ܥܬܰܠܝܳܐ ܒܰܪܬܳܐ ܕܥܶܡܪܝܺ ܡܰܠܟܳܐ ܕܐܚܺܝܣܪܳܝܶܠ. ܘܐܶܬܢܰܛܪܰܬ ܛܽܘܦܣܳܬܳܐ ܒܝܘܽܐܳܫ ܒܰܪ ܐܰܚܰܙܝܳܐ: ܘܠܳܐ ܐܶܬܩܛܶܠ ܥܰܡ ܐܰܚܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܟܰܕ݂ ܐܰܘܒܰܕ݂ ܥܬܰܠܝܳܐ ܙܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܡܰܠܟܘܽܬܳܐ ܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܚܙܳܬ ܕܐܶܬܩܛܶܠ ܐܰܚܰܙܝܳܐ ܒܪܳܗ ܡܶܢ ܐܝܺܕܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܕܝܳܗܘܽ ܫܰܪܺܝܪܳܐܝܺܬ. ܘܐܶܫܰܕ݂ ܕܡܳܐ ܙܰܟܳܝܐ. ܘܰܠܘܳܬ ܛܰܝܒܘܽܬܳܐ ܕܝܘܽܝܳܕ݂ܳܥ ܟܳܗܢܳܝܐ ܘܐܰܘܒܶܕ݂ ܠܙܰܪܥܶܗ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܪܶܗ: ܘܡܶܛܽܠ ܒܘܽܪܟ݁ܬܳܐ ܐܶܬܢܛܰܪ ܙܰܪܥܶܗ. ܘܩܳܡ ܐܰܡܨܺܝܳܐ ܘܰܕܒܰܪ ܡܰܠܟܘܽܬܳܐ: ܘܰܚܛܳܝܐ ܒܰܦܬܰܟܪܶܐ ܣܓܶܕ ܠܰܐܠܳܗܶܐ ܐ̱ܚܪ̈ܳܢܶܐ ܢܘܽܟܪ̈ܳܢܶܝܐ: ܘܡܶܛܽܠ ܒܘܽܪܟ݁ܬܳܐ ܐܶܬܢܛܰܪ ܙܰܪܥܶܗ. ܘܩܳܡ ܥܘܽܙܺܝܳܐ ܒܳܬܪܶܗ ܘܐܰܡܪܰܕ݂ ܥܰܠ ܟܳܗܢܘܽܬܳܐ ܘܠܳܐ ܫܰܡܥܳܗ: ܘܰܡܚܳܝܗ̱ܝ ܩܰܕ݂ܝܺܫܳܝܐ ܒܰܡܚܘܽܬܳܐ ܕܓܰܪܒܳܐ ܟܽܠܗܘܽܢ ܝܰܘ̈ܡܳܬܶܗ. ܘܩܳܡ ܝܘܽܬܳܡ ܒܡܰܠܟ݁ܘܽܬܳܐ
with his army. The blessing of the righteous burned in Asa; he did justice, and God heard his prayer, and his enemies were delivered into his hands. The blessing was preserved in Jehoshaphat, who did what was right before the Lord. But Jehoram multiplied sins; yet because of the blessing, he was preserved, and although he sinned, he was not blotted out like the house of Ahab, to which he was related by marriage; and Athaliah, the daughter of Omri, king of Israel, seduced him. But the grain was preserved in Joash, the son of Ahaziah, who was not killed with his brothers when Athaliah destroyed the royal seed, seeing her own son Ahaziah killed by Jehu, the son of Nimshi. Joash, however, shed innocent blood and acted ungratefully toward Jehoiada the priest, extinguishing his progeny after him; yet because of the blessing, his own seed was preserved. And Amaziah arose and governed the kingdom; but he sinned with idols and worshipped other foreign gods, yet his seed was preserved because of the blessing; and Uzziah arose after him, who, rebelling against the priesthood, did not obey; therefore, the Most Holy struck him with the plague of leprosy all his days. But Jotham obtained the kingdom, who sinned much,


[p. 50] {#po.0.p50}
ܘܐܰܣܓܝܼ ܠܡܶܚܛܳܐ: ܘܡܶܛܽܠ ܒܘܽܪܟ݁ܬ݁ܳܐ ܙܰܪܥܶܗ ܐܶܬܢܛܰܪ. ܘܩܳܡ ܐܳܚܳܙ ܒܡܰܠܟܘܽܬܳܐ ܘܐܰܣܓܝܼ ܠܡܶܚܛܳܐ ܘܰܣܓ݁ܶܕ݂ ܠܰܐܠܳܗܶܐ ܡ̈ܺܝܬܶܐ ܘܐܶܡܰܪ: ܐܰܢ̱ܬܘܽܢ ܐܰܠܳܗܰܝ ܘܡܳܪ̈ܰܝ ܘܰܠܟܘܽܢ ܣܶܓ݁ܶܕ݂ ܐܹܢܳܐ: ܘܡܶܛܽܠ ܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܐܶܬܢܛܰܪ ܙܰܪܥܶܗ. ܘܩܳܡ ܚܶܙܩܝܳܐ ܡܰܠܟܳܐ ܙܰܕ݂ܝܺܩܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܟ݀ܽܠܶܗ ܝܗܽܘܕܳܐ ܒܐܘܽܪܺܫܠܶܡ: ܘܨܰܠܝܼ ܘܐܶܬ݂ܚܰܢܰܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܐܰܠܳܗܶܗ: ܘܐܶܫܬܰܡܥܰܬ ܨܠܘܳܬܶܗ ܘܚܰܪܒܰܬ ܒܥܶܠܕܒܳܒܰܘܗ̱ܝ: ܘܫܰܡܶܫ ܙܰܗܝܳܐܝܺܬ ܒܘܽܪܟ݁ܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ
but because of the blessing, his offspring was preserved. Then Ahaz succeeded to the kingdom, who also sinned much and worshipped dead gods and said: 'You are my gods and my lords, and I worship you.' But because of the blessing, his seed was preserved, and Hezekiah, a righteous king, reigned over all Judah in Jerusalem; he prayed and supplicated before his God, and his prayer was heard, and it itself routed his enemies. He used the blessing of the righteous which he had in himself gloriously; for he fell ill, prayed, and was relieved of his disease.


[p. 51] {#po.0.p51}


[p. 52] {#po.0.p52}


[p. 53] {#po.0.p53}
ܕܰܒܓܰܘܶܗ. ܘܐܶܬܟܝܪܰܗ ܘܨܰܠܝܳ ܘܐܶܬ݂ܚܠܶܝܡ ܡܶܢ ܟܘܽܪܳܗܳܢܶܗ܀ ܘܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܐܚܺܝܕ݂ ܐ݈ܢܳܫܺܝܢ ܚܰܒܶܒ݂ܝ ܕܡܰܝܬ݁ܶܝܢ ܥܶܕ݂ܠܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܚܶܙܩܝܳܐ: ܕܰܠܡܳܢܳܐ ܟܶܪܝܰܬ ܠܶܗ ܒܟܘܽܪܗܳܢܶܗ ܕܡܳܝܬܶܬ݂ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܕ݁ܗܳܐ ܙܰܕ݂ܝܺܩܘܽܬܳܐ ܪܰܒ݁ܬ݁ܳܐ ܦܠܰܚ ܩܕܳܡ ܐܰܠܳܗܶܗ ܘܡܳܢܳܐ ܠܰܡ ܗܶܢܝܳܢܳܐ ܡܛܝܗ̱ܝ ܒ݁ܗܳܝ ܐܰܘܣܶܦܬܶܗ ܕܐܶܬܬܣܰܝܒܰܬ ܒܶܗ ܡܘܽܡܳܐ ܒܗܳܝ ܬܰܘܣܶܦܬܶܗ ܥܰܠ ܕܐܶܫܬܰܒ݁ܗܰܪ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܒܥܘܽܬܪܶܗ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܐܝܺܙܓܰܕ݁ܶܐ ܕ݁ܰܡܠܶܟ݂ ܒܳܒܶܠ ܘܠܳܐ ܫܰܒܰܚ ܫܡܳܐ ܕܐܰܠܳܗܶܗ ܕܐܰܚܠܡܶܡܗ ܡܶܢ ܟܘܽܪܗܳܢܶܗ. ܥܰܠ ܗܳܕܶܐ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܟܶܪܝܰܬ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܠܶܗ ܠܚܶܙܩܝܳܐ ܡܰܠܟܳܐ ܙܰܕ݂ܝܺܩܳܐ ܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܰܥܕ݂ܰܟܝܺܠ ܒ݁ܪܳܐ ܠܰܝܬ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܠܶܗ: ܘܰܒܚܰܨܶܗ ܛܡܝܼܪܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܬ
18. But there are men found, most beloved, who criticize and blame Hezekiah, saying: 'Why did he grieve so bitterly that he was succumbing to disease, he who had performed excellent justice before his God? And what fruit did he perceive from that increase, from which he incurred more ruin, when he boasted of his riches before the envoys of the king of Babylon, but did not glorify the name of his God, who had cured him of his infirmity?' But Hezekiah, the righteous king, was surely suffering because he did not yet have a son, and the blessing of the righteous was hidden within himself; and for this reason, groaning, he said: 'The blessing in all the ancient ages, from Adam until
ܒܘܽܪܟ݁ܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܙܰܕܺܝܺܩܶܐ. ܘܗܳܟ݁ܰܢܳܐ ܐܶܡܰܪ ܒܰܚܢܰܢܬܶܗ: ܕܒܘܽܪܟ݁ܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܰܪܕܳܐ ܒܟܽܠܗܘܽܢ ܕܳܪܶܐ ܩܰܕ݁̈ܡܳܝܐ ܡܶܢ ܐܳܕ݂ܳܡ ܘܰܠܘܳܬܝ ܒܐܰܪܒܥܝܢ ܘܫܬ ܫܰܪ̈ܒ݁ܳܢ: ܡܶܢܝ ܠܡܳܢܳܐ ܠܳܐ ܢܶܦܩܰܬ ܘܡܶܢܝ ܕܶܥܟܰܬ ܛܘܽܦܣܺܝܬܳܐ܆ ܗܳܐ ܡܶܟܝܺܠ ܡܶܬܝܽܗܽܒ ܠܶܗ ܣܓܘܽܠܳܐ ܠܰܚܒܳܠܳܐ. ܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܗܳܠܶܝܢ ܐܶܬܪܰܥܝܺ ܘܨܰܠܝܺ ܚܢܢܳܓ݁ܺܐܝܺܬ: ܐܶܬܳܐ ܠܘܳܬܶܗ ܐܶܫܰܥܝܳܐ ܢܒܺܝܳܐ ܘܐܶܡܰܪ ܠܶܗ: ܗܳܟܰܢܳܐ ܐܳܡܰܪ ܡܳܪܝܳܐ: ܗܳܐ ܡܰܘܣܶܦ ܐ̱ܢܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܫܢܰܝ̈ܟܶ: ܚܰܡܫܰܥܣܪ̈ܶܐ ܫܢܺܝܢ ܚܳܝܶܐ ܐܢ̱ܬ. ܘܐܰܘܣܶܦ ܠܶܗ ܚܰܡܫܰܥܣܪ̈ܶܐ ܫܢܺܝܢ. ܗܳܝܝܕ݁ܶܝܢ ܒܰܫܢܰܬ ܬܡܳܢܰܥܣܪ̈ܶܐ ܕ݁ܡܰܠܟܘܽܬܶܗ ܘܒܰܫܢܰܬ ܬܠܳܬ ܕܐܰܘܣܶܦܬܶܗ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܰܡܢܰܫܶܐ. ܥܶܣܪܝܺܢ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܘܰܬܫܰܥ ܫܢܺܝܢ ܐܰܡܠܶܟ: ܩܰܕ݂̈ܡܳܝܳܬܳܐ ܐܰܪܒ݁ܰܥܣܪ̈ܶܐ ܘܰܐܚܪ̈ܳܝܳܬ݁ܳܐ ܚܰܡܫܰܥܣܪ̈ܶܐ ܘܫܶܠܡܰܬ ܡܶܠܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܰܟܬܝܺܒܳܐ: ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܢܰܓܪܘܽܢ ܝܰܘ̈ܡܳܬܗܘܽܢ: ܘܰܫܢܰܝ̈ܗܘܽܢ ܕܥܰܘܳܠܶܐ ܢܶܬܒܰܨܪܳܢ. ܐܶܬܒܨܰܪܺܝ ܫܢ̈ܰܝܳܐ ܕ݁ܕܳܪܳܐ ܡܚܰܒܠܳܢܳܐ ܕܰܒܝܰܘ̈ܡܰܝ ܢܘܽܚ ܘܰܕ݂ܡܰܠܟ݁ܘܽܬܳܐ ܚܰܛܺܝܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܒܶܝܬ ܐܶܦܪܶܝܡ: ܘܐܰܘܣܶܦ ܠܚܶܙܩܝܳܐ ܘܐܳܦ ܠܐܝܽܘܒ: ܠܚܶܙܩܝܳܐ ܐܰܘܣܶܝܦ ܚܰܝܡܫܰܥܣܪ̈ܶܐ ܦܥ̈ܚܢ ܘܠܐܝܽܘܽܒ ܐܰܥܦܳܐ ܒܰܫܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܘܰܒܩܶܢܝܳܢܶܗ ܘܒܰܒܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ. ܘܐܶܢ ܐ̱ܢܳܫ ܢܶܬܚܰܪܙܶܐ ܕܠܳܐ ܩܰܒܶܠ ܐܝܽܘܽܒ ܐܰܥܦܳܐ ܒܟܽܠ ܡܶܕ݁ܶܡ: ܢܶܬܛܰܦܰܣ ܕܐ ܚܶܪܟ݁ܳܢܳܐ. ܗܳܟ݁ܰܢ݁ܳܐ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܟܬܝܺܒ: ܕܐܰܣܓܝ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܐ݈ܚܪܳܝܬܶܗ ܕܐܝܽܘܽܒ ܡܶܢ ܩܰܕ݂ܡܳܝܬܶܗ: ܘܐܶܬܚܺܠܶܕ݂ܘ ܠܶܗ ܫܰܒܥܳܐ ܒܢܺܝ̈ܢ ܘܰܬܠܳܬ
me, has flowed down through forty-six generations; why then will it depart from me? Why will the grain be diminished from me henceforth? Behold, is the cluster being handed over to destruction?' Furthermore, as he was thinking such things and praying tearfully, Isaiah the prophet arrived, who said to him: 'Thus says the Lord: Behold, I will add to your years: you will live fifteen years.' Therefore, He added fifteen years to him, and in the eighteenth year of his reign, namely the third of his prolonged life, he begat Manasseh. For he reigned twenty-nine years: first indeed fourteen years, and lastly fifteen, and the word which is written was fulfilled: 'The righteous will prolong their days, and the years of the wicked will be shortened.' Indeed, in the days of Noah, the years of the perverse generation were shortened, just as the years of the sinful house of Ephraim were shortened; but those of Hezekiah and Job were prolonged: to Hezekiah He added fifteen years, and Job obtained double in his years, possessions, and children. But if anyone raises a controversy about the fact that Job did not receive double in everything, let him allow himself to be taught without contention. For it is so written: 'The Lord multiplied the latter days of Job more than his beginnings, and there were born to him seven sons and three daughters.' And the seven sons


[p. 54] {#po.0.p54}


[p. 55] {#po.0.p55}


[p. 56] {#po.0.p56}
ܩܰܕ̈ܡܳܝܶܐ ܘܰܐܚܪ̈ܳܢܶܝܐ ܟܰܕ݂ ܗܳܘܶܐ ܙܰܒ݂ܢܳܐ ܕ݁ܰܩܝܳܡܬ݁ܳܐ
and three daughters, together with the latter ones, he will receive in double number at the time of the resurrection in the world of the righteous; his
ܒܙܰܕܺܝܩܶܐ: ܘܟܰܝܕ݂ ܗܘܳܐ ܒܗܳܢܳܐ ܥܳܠܡܳܐ ܩܰܒܶܠ ܩܶܢܝܳܢܶܗ ܒܟܽܠ ܡܶܕ݁ܶܡ ܐܰܥܦܳܝܐ: ܘܰܡܣܰܝܟܬܳܐ ܠܰܢ ܡܶܠܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܐܳܦ ܫܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܐܰܥܦܳܐ ܝܰܗ̱ܒ ܠܶܗ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܐܰܟ ܕܰܟܬܝܺܒ: ܕܣܶܐܒ ܐܝܽܘܽܒ ܘܰܣܒܰܥ ܝܰܘ̈ܡܳܬ݂ܶܗ ܘܡܺܝܬ. ܒܳܬ݁ܰܪ ܢܶܣܝܽܘܢܶܗ ܡܳܐܐ ܘܐܰܪܒܥܺܝܢ ܫܢ̈ܺܝܢ ܚܝܳܐ: ܘܰܕ݂ܡܶܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܢܶܣܝܽܘܢܶܗ
entire possessions, when he was in this world, he obtained in double; and finally, it is clear to us that the years of his life were doubled for him by God; for it is so written: 'Job grew old, and was satisfied with his days, and died.' He lived one hundred and forty years after his trial, but seventy before the trial. And the word which is written was fulfilled over him: 'Job received double in everything; in his years and possessions in this world, but in sons and daughters in the world of the righteous.' For it is in the power of the craftsman to increase and contract his
ܡܶܠܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܰܟܬܝܺܒܳܐ: ܕܩܰܒܶܠ ܐܝܽܘܽܒ ܐܰܥܦܳܝܐ ܒܟܽܠ ܡܶܕ݁ܶܡ: ܫܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ
creation.


[p. 57] {#po.0.p57}
ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܓ݁ܶܝܪ ܐܘܰܡܳܢܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܦܓ݂ܰܪܬܶܗ ܠܡܽܘܣܳܟܽܘ ܘܠܡܰܒܪܳܪܽܘ܀ ܘܐܶܬܝܺܠܶܕ݂ ܡܢܰܫܶܐ ܝܰܠܕ݂ܳܐ ܪܰܫܺܝܥܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܓ݁ܰܒܪܳܐ ܙܰܕܝܺܩܳܐ: ܘܐܰܣܓܝܺ ܠܢܶܡܛܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܟܽܠܗܘܽܢ ܕܰܩܕ݂ܳܡܰܘܗ̱ܝ. ܒܰܪ ܬܰܪܬܰܥܣܪ̈ܶܐ ܫܢ̈ܺܝܢ
19. But Manasseh was born, the wicked son of a righteous father; and he surpassed all his predecessors in the number of his sins. He obtained the kingdom at twelve years of age, and reigned fifty-five years. But although he had multiplied iniquities
ܐܰܡܠܶܟ. ܘܐܳܦܶܢ ܐܰܣܓܝܰ ܚܛܳܗܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܘܰܣܓ݁ܶܕ݂ ܠܰܦܬܰܟܪ̈ܶܐ: ܡܶܛܺܝܠ ܒܘܽܪܟ݁ܬܳܐ ܐܶܬܢܛܰܪ ܙܰܪܥܶܗ. ܘܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܐܳܡܘܽܢ. ܘܐܰܡܠܶܟ݂ ܐܰܡܘܽܢ ܘܗܰܠܶܟ ܒܐܘܽܪ̈ܚܳܬܶܗ ܕܰܡܢܰܫܶܐ: ܘܡܶܛܽܠ ܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܕܒܶܗ ܕܰܒܰܪ ܡܰܡܠܟܘܽܬܳܐ. ܘܐܶܬܝܺܠܶܕ݂
and worshipped idols, his seed was preserved because of the blessing. He begat Amon, who, when he had reigned, walked in the ways of Manasseh; but because of the


[p. 58] {#po.0.p58}
ܡܶܝܢܶܗ ܓܰܒܪܳܐ ܙܰܕ݂ܝܺܩܳܝܐ ܚܘܽܫ̈ܰܫܳܐ ܕܘܰܟ݂ܪܳܢܶܗ ܠܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ: ܕܐܰܥܒܰܝܪ ܛܰܝܡܐܘܳܬܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܐܝܺܣܪܳܝܶܠ ܒܚܰܘܽܡܰܝ ܡܰܠܟܘܽܬܶܗ.
blessing that was in him, he nevertheless governed the kingdom. And from him was born Josiah, whose memory is for a blessing, who took away uncleanness from the land of Israel in the days of his reign. But when the sins of the people pressed, he was gathered before


[p. 59] {#po.0.p59}
ܕ݁ܥܰܡܳܝܐ ܐܶܬܟܰܢܰܫ ܡܶܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܒܚܺܝܳܫܬܳܐ: ܘܠܳܐ ܐܶܬܳܬ ܒܝܰܘܡܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܥܰܠ ܥܰܡܳܝܐ ܐܰܟ ܕܐܶܬ݁ܢܰܒܝܰܬ ܚܘܽܠܕܝܺ ܢ ܒܚܰܬܳܐ ܟܰܕ݂ ܬܰܚܕܰܪ ܠܘܳܬܳܗ ܠܐܫ ܐܰܠ ܦ݁ܶܬܓ݂ܺܝܡܳܐ ܘܫܰܠܚܰܝܬ ܠܶܗ: ܕܗܳܟ݁ܰܢܳܐ ܐܳܡܰܪ ܡܳܪܝܳܐ ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܕܐܝܺܣܪܳܝܶܠ: ܚܠܳܦ ܕܐܶܬܬܒܰܪ ܠܶܒܳܟ
the evil happened, which was not brought upon the people in his days; for Huldah the prophetess had prophesied this when he sent to her to inquire, and she commanded him: 'Thus says the Lord God of Israel: Because your heart was broken, and you grieved for the sins of the people when you heard the words of the law; behold, I will gather you to your fathers, and you will not see the evil that will come upon this people.' And he was taken away from the face of the evil, when, namely, Pharaoh Necho, king of Egypt, killed him. Jehoahaz, however,
ܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܫܡܰܥܬ ܦܶܬܓ݂ܺܡ̈ܶܝܐ ܕܢܳܡܘܽܣܳܝܐ: ܗܳܐ ܟܳܢܶܫ ܐ̱ܢܳܐ ܠܳܟ݂ ܥܰܡ ܐܰܒܳܗܰܝܺܟ ܘܠܳܐ ܬ݁ܶܚܙܶܐ ܒܝܺܫܬܳܐ ܕܐܳܬܝܳܐ ܥܰܠ ܥܰܡܳܐ ܗܳܢܳܐ. ܘܐܶܬܟܰܢܰܫ ܡܶܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܒܚܳܫܬܳܐ ܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܩܰܛܠܶܗ ܦ݁ܰܪܥܘܽܢ ܚܓܺܝܪܳܐ ܡܰܠܟ݁ܳܐ ܕܡܶܨܪܶܝܢ. ܘܩܳܡ ܒܡܰܠܟܘܽܬܳܐ ܝܳܘܐܚܳܙ ܒܪܶܗ: ܘܡܶܛܽܠ ܕܠܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܬ ܒܗ ܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܠܳܐ ܐܰܦܫܰܝܪ ܒܡܰܠܟܘܽܬܳܐ ܕܒܶܝܬ ܕܰܘܝܺܝܕ݂. ܐܰܡܠܶܟ݂ ܬܠܳܬܳܐ ܝܰܪ̈ܚܝܺܢ ܘܡܶܢ ܒܳܬ݁ܰܪܟ݁ܶܢ ܕ݁ܰܒܪܶܗ ܦܿܪܥܘܽܢ ܚܓܺܝܪܳܐ ܡܰܠܟܳܐ ܕܡܶܨܪܶܝܢ ܘܡܺܝܬ ܒܡܶܨܪܶܝܢ ܘܐܶܬܥܛܺܝ ܕܘܰܟ݂ܪܳܢܶܗ.
his son, obtained the kingdom; but because the blessing was not in him, it did not turn out prosperously in the kingdom of the house of David. He reigned three months; after which Pharaoh Necho, king of Egypt, took him away, and he died in Egypt, and his memory was blotted out. But Jehoiakim obtained the kingdom, in whom the blessing of the righteous was hidden; and in the third year of his reign, Nebuchadnezzar subdued him with tribute, who also
ܘܐܰܡܠܶܟ ܝܘܽܝܳܩܺܝܡ ܒ݁ܡܰܠܟ݁ܘܽܬܳܐ ܕܒ݁ܶܗ ܛܡܺܝܪܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܬ ܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ: ܘܒܰܫܢܰܬ ܬܠܳܬ ܕܡܰܠܟܘܽܬܶܗ ܟܰܒܫܶܗ ܢܒܘܽܟܰܕ݂ܢܳܨܰܪ ܒ݁ܡܰܕ݂ܰܬܳܐ ܘܰܕܒܰܪ ܠܕܳܢܺܝܐܶܝܠ ܘܠܰܐܚܰܝܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܒܰܚܡܰܬ̈ܐ: ܘܰܦܠܰܚ ܠܰܢܒܘܽܟܰܕ݂ܢܳܨܰܪ
took Daniel and his brothers as hostages; he served Nebuchadnezzar for eight years, and Nebuchadnezzar, as he was multiplying sins and rebelling,


[p. 60] {#po.0.p60}
ܘܰܡܪܰܕ݂ ܩܰܛܠܶܗ ܢܒܘܽܟܰܕ݂ܢܳܨܰܪ: ܘܓ݁ܰܪܘ ܘܰܦܕܰܐܘܽܗ̱ܝ ܠܒܰܪ ܡܶܢ ܐܰܪ̈ܥܶܝܐ ܕܐܘܽܪܰܫܠܶܡ: ܘܰܩܒܘܽܪܰܐ ܚܡܳܪܳܐ ܐܶܬܩܒܰܪ. ܘܐܰܡܠܶܟ ܝܘܽܚܳܟܝܳܢ ܒܪܗ ܒܳܬ݂ܪܶܗ: ܘܥܰܠ ܕܒܶܝܗ ܛܡܺܝܪܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ ܐܰܡܠܶܟ݂ ܬܠܳܬ݁ܳܐ ܝܰܪ̈ܚܝܺܢ: ܘܟܰܕ݂ ܘܪܰܕ݂ ܥܰܠ ܡܰܠܟܳܐ ܕܒܳܒܶܠ ܐܶܬܰܘ ܥܠܰܘܗ̱ܝ ܚܰܝܠܰܘ̈ܬܳܐ ܕ݁ܟ݁ܰܠܺܕ݂ܳܝܝܐ ܘܕܰܒܪܘܽܗ̱ܝ ܘܐܺܙܰܠܘ ܠܒܳܒܶܠ: ܘܐܶܬܐܳܣܰܪ ܬܠܳܬܝܺܢ ܘܰܫܒܰܥ ܫܢ̈ܬܝܢ ܒܒܳܒܶܠ ܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܛܡܺܝܪܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܒܶܗ ܒܘܽܪܟ݁ܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝܺܩܶܐ. ܘܐܶܫܬܒܶܩ ܥܰܡܳܝܐ ܒܐܘܪܺܝܫܠܶܡ ܡܶܢ ܛܰܝܡܐܘܽܬܳܐ: ܘܩܳܡ ܥܠܰܝܗܘܽܢ ܨܶܕܩܺܝܐ ܥܰܠ ܫܰܪܟ݁ܳܝܐ ܕܥܰܡܳܝܐ ܒܡܰܠܟܘܽܬܳܐ. ܘܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܝܘܺܝܳܟܝ݁ܢ ܠܫܶܠܰܬ݁ܺܝܐܶܝܠ ܒܒܳܒܶܠ. ܘܰܟܡܳܐ ܕܒܘܽܪܟ݂ܬ݁ܳܐ ܒܒܳܒܶܠ ܗܘܳܬ ܕܒܰܪܘ ܒܳܒ݁̈ܠܳܝܐ ܡܰܠܟ݁ܘܽܬܳܐ ܒܫܰܝܢܳܐ ܘܒ݁ܰܫܠܳܡܳܐ. ܘܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܐܶܬܚܰܠܶܕ݂ ܫܶܠܰܬ݁ܺܝܐܶܝܠ ܒܐܰܪܥܳܐ ܕ݁ܒܳܒܶܠ:
killed him; dragging him, they threw him outside the gates of Jerusalem, and he was buried with the burial of an ass. But Jehoiachin his son reigned after him; and because the blessing of the righteous was hidden in him, he reigned three months; and when he had rebelled against the king of Babylon, the armies of the Chaldeans went out against him, by whom they were taken away and went to Babylon, and there he remained in chains for thirty-seven years, he in whom the blessing of the righteous was hidden; but the people were left in Jerusalem because of uncleanness, and Zedekiah was established as king over the remaining people. Jehoiachin, however, begat Shealtiel in Babylon. And as long as the blessing remained in Babylon, the Babylonians administered the kingdom in tranquility and peace. But when Shealtiel was born in the land of Babylon, the blessing of the righteous was hidden in him. Shealtiel begat Zerubbabel. Furthermore, in the days of Zerubbabel, the migration from Babylon was turned back by Ezra the scribe, in the reign of Cyrus the Persian, and Zerubbabel [returned] with the migration, and became
ܒܶܗ ܛܡܺܝܪܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܒܘܽܪܟ݁ܬ݁ܳܐ ܕܙܰܕ̈ܝ̈ܩܶܐ. ܘܫܶܠܬܺܐܶܝܠ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܙܘܽܪܒܳܒܶܠ. ܘܗܳܝܕ݁ܶܝܢ ܒܚܰܝܘܽܡܳܬܶܗ ܕܙܘܽܪܒܳܒܶܠ ܗܶܦܟܰܬ ܠܳܗ ܫܒܝܽܬܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܒܶܠ ܒܚܰܕ݂ ܥܰܙܪܳܐ ܣܳܦܪܳܐ ܒܡܰܠܟܘܽܬܳܐ ܕܟܘܽܪܶܫ ܦܳܪܣܳܝܳܐ: ܘܙܘܽܪܒܳܒܶܠ ܥܰܡܳܗ ܕܰܒܚܺܝܬܳܐ ܘܰܗܘܳܐ ܥܠܰܝܗܽܘܢ ܐܰܟ ܕܐܶܡܰܪ ܢܒܺܝܳܐ: ܕܙܘܽܪܒܳܒܶܠ ܒܰܪ ܫܶܠܬܺܐܺܝܠ ܪܰܒ݁ܳܐ ܕܝܺܗܘܽܕܳܐ: ܘܝܶܫܘܽܥ ܒܰܪ ܝܘܽܙܕܳܩ ܟܳܗܢܳܐ ܪܰܒ݁ܳܐ. ܗܳܠܶܝܢ ܐܶܢܘܽܢ ܬܪܶܝܢ ܒܢܰܝ̈ ܙܰܝܬܳܐ ܕܩܳܝܡܝܺܢ ܗ̱ܘܰܘ ܩܕܳܡ ܡܳܪܳܐ ܕܟ݁ܽܠܳܗ ܐܰܪܥܳܐ܀
their leader, as the prophet said: 'Zerubbabel the son of Shealtiel, prince of Judah, and Joshua the son of Jozadak, the high priest. These are the two sons of oil, who stood by the Lord of the whole earth.'


[p. 61] {#po.0.p61}


[p. 62] {#po.0.p62}
ܘܟܰܕ ܦܢܳܬ ܒܘܽܪܟܬܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܒܶܠ ܠܐܘܽܪܺܫܠܶܡ ܒܶܛܠܰܬ ܡܰܠܟ݁ܘܽܬܳܐ ܕܒܳܒ̈ܠܳܝܶܐ ܘܐܰܡܠܶܟ݂ ܟܘܽܪܶܫ ܦܳܪܣܳܝܳܐ. ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܫܬܝܺܢ ܘܰܬܠܳܬ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ ܦܢܳܬ ܒܘܽܪܟ݂ܬ݁ܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܒܶܠ ܠܐܘܽܪܺܫܠܶܡ: ܡܶܢ ܙܰܒ݂ܢܳܐ ܕܐܶܫܬܒܝ ܝܘܺܝܳܟܝܺܢ ܥܕ݂ܰܡܳܐ ܕܗܶܦܟܰܬ ܫܒܝܽܬܳܐ ܒܚܰܕ݂ ܥܰܙܪܳܐ̇ ܘܙܘܽܪܒܳܒܶܠ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܐܰܒܝܽܘܽܕ݂: ܘܐܰܒ݂ܝܘܽܕ݂ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܶܐܠܝܰܩܝܺܡ: ܘܐܶܠܝܰܩܝܺܡ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܥܳܙܘܽܪ: ܘܥܳܙܘܽܪ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܨܳܕܘܽܩ: ܘܨܳܕܘܽܩ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܐܵܟܝܺܢ: ܘܐܵܟܝܺܢ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܶܐܠܝܽܘܽܕ݂: ܘܐܶܠܝܽܘܽܕ݂ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܶܐܠܝܰܥܙܰܪ: ܘܐܶܠܝܰܥܙܰܪ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܡܰܬܰܢ: ܘܡܰܬܰܢ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ: ܘܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܝܰܘܣܶܦ: ܘܝܰܘܣܺܝܦ ܐܶܬܩܪܝَ ܐܰܒܳܐ ܠܝܶܫܘܽܥ ܡܫܺܝܚܳܐ: ܘܝܶܫܘܽܥ ܐܶܬܝܺܠܶܕ݂ ܡܶܢ ܡܰܪܝܰܡ ܒܬܘܽܠܬܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܙܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܒܶܝܬ ܕܰܘܝܺܕ݂ ܡܶܢ ܪܘܽܚܳܐ ܕܩܘܽܕܫܳܐ ܐܰܟ ܕܰܟܬܝܺܒ: ܕ݁ܝܰܘܣܶܦ ܘܡܰܪܝܰܡ ܡܟܝܺܪܬܶܗ ܬܪ̈ܰܝܗܘܽܢ ܡܶܢ ܒܰܝܬܶܗ ܕܕܰܘܝܺܕ݂. ܘܰܫܠܺܝܚܳܐ ܣܳܗܶܕ݂: ܕܝܶܫܘܰܥ ܡܫܺܝܚܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܡܰܪܝܰܡ ܗܘܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܙܰܪܥܳܐ ܕܒܶܝܬ ܕܰܘܝܺܕ݂ ܒܪܘܽܚܳܐ ܕܩܘܽܕܫَܳܐ. ܝܰܘܣܶܦ ܐܶܬܩܪܝܺ ܐܰܒ݁ܳܐ ܠܝܶܫܘܽܥ ܟܰܕ ܠܳܐ ܗ̱ܘܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܙܰܪܥܶܗ ܐܶܬܝܺܠܶܕ݂. ܐܶܠܳܐ ܫܡܳܐ ܕܐܰܒܳܗܘܽܬ݁ܳܐ ܐܶܬܝܰܗܰܒ
20. But when the blessing returned from Babylon to Jerusalem, the kingdom of the Babylonians ceased, and Cyrus the Persian reigned. And the blessing returned from Babylon to Jerusalem sixty-three years after Jehoiachin was taken away, until the captivity was brought back by the help of Ezra. Zerubbabel begat Abiud, Abiud begat Eliakim, Eliakim begat Azor, Azor begat Zadok, Zadok begat Achim, Achim begat Eliud, Eliud begat Eleazar, Eleazar begat Matthan, Matthan begat Jacob, Jacob begat Joseph, and Joseph was called the father of Jesus Christ; but Jesus was born of the Virgin Mary, from the seed of the house of David, of the Holy Spirit, as it is written: 'Joseph with Mary betrothed to him, both of the house of David.' And the Apostle testifies that Jesus Christ was from Mary, from the seed of David, in the Holy Spirit. Joseph was called the father of Jesus, although He was not born of his seed; but the name of fatherhood was derived from Adam to Joseph through sixty-three generations.


[p. 63] {#po.0.p63}


[p. 64] {#po.0.p64}
ܡܶܢ ܐܳܕ݁ܳܡ ܥܕ݂ܰܡܳܐ ܠܝܰܘܣܶܦ ܠܶܫܬܝܺܢ ܘܰܬܠܳܬ ܫܰܪ̈ܒ݁ܳܢ. ܘܐܶܫܬ݁ܩܶܠ ܫܡܳܐ ܕܐܰܒܳܗܘܽܬܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܝܰܘܣܶܦ ܘܐܶܬܬܣܺܝܡ ܥܰܠ ܡܫܺܝܚܳܐ: ܡܶܢ ܝܰܘܣܶܦ ܩܰܒܶܠ ܫܡܳܐ ܕܐܰܒܳܗܘܽܬܳܐ: ܘܡܶܢ ܝܘܽܚܰܢܳܢ ܫܡܳܐ ܕܟܳܗܢܘܽܬܳܐ: ܘܡܶܢ ܡܰܪܝܰܡ ܠܒܶܫ ܦ݁ܰܓ݁ܪܳܐ ܘܩܰܒܶܠ ܫܡܳܐ ܕܡܰܘܠܳܕ݂ܳܐ. ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܫܬܝܺܢ ܘܰܬܪܶܝܢ ܫܰܒ݁ܽܘܥܺܝܢ ܐܶܬܝܺܠܶܕ݂ ܡܫܺܝܚܳܐ ܘܐܶܬܩܛܶܠ: ܘܐܶܫܬܰܩܠܰܬ ܛܘܽܒܬܳܐ ܕܒܘܽܪܟܬ݁ܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܣܓܘܽܠܳܐ: ܘܐܶܬܝܺܗܶܒ ܟ݁ܽܠܶܗ ܣܓܘܽܠܳܐ ܠܰܚܒܳܠܳܐ. ܘܰܚܪܶܒ ܟܰܪܡܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܕܠܳܐ
The name of fatherhood was taken from Joseph and imposed upon Christ. Therefore, He received the name of fatherhood from Joseph, the name of priesthood from John, and from Mary He put on a body and received the name of generation. After sixty-two weeks, Christ was born and killed; the grain of the blessing was taken from the cluster, and the whole cluster was handed over to destruction; the vineyard, deprived of the plowman, was devastated, and it brought forth wild grapes and fruits of wormwood, the vine was overturned, and the branch was eradicated.


[p. 65] {#po.0.p65}
ܦܘܽܠܚܳܢ̈ܶܐ ܘܐܰܘܥܶܫ ܚܰܕ݁ܽܘܕ݂ܶܐ ܘܦܺܐܪ̈ܶܐ ܡܪܺܝܪ̈ܶܐ: ܘܚܶܪܒܰܬ ܓ݁ܦܶܬܳܐ ܘܐܶܬܥܰܩܪܰܬ ܣܰܬܳܐ܀ ܡܶܟ݂ܝܺܠ ܩܰܒܶܠ ܚܘܽܫܒܳܢܳܐ ܕܗܳܠܶܝܢ ܫܬܝܺܢ ܘܰܬܠܳܬ ܫܰܪ̈ܒ݁ܳܢ ܡܶܢ ܐܳܕ݁ܳܡ ܥܕܰܡܳܐ ܕܐܶܬܝܺܠܶܕ݂ ܡܫܺܝܚܳܐ. ܐܳܕܳܡ: ܫܶܬ: ܐܶܢܘܽܫ: ܩܰܝܢܳܢ: ܡܰܗܠܰܠܶܐܝܠ: ܝܳܪܶܕ݂: ܚܢܘܽܟ: ܡܰܬܘܽܫܠܰܚ: ܠܳܡܶܟ: ܢܘܽܚ: ܫܶܡ: ܐܰܪܦܰܟܫܰܕ: ܫܳܠܰܚ: ܥܶܒܶܪ: ܦܳܠܶܓ: ܪܰܥܘܽ: ܣܪܘܽܓ: ܢܳܚܘܽܪ: ܬܶܪܰܚ: ܐܰܒܪܳܗܳܡ: ܐܝܰܣܚܳܩ: ܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ: ܝܺܗܘܽܕܳܐ: ܦܳܪܶܨ: ܚܶܨܪܽܘܽܢ: ܐܳܪܳܡ: ܥܰܡܺܝܢܳܕܳܒ: ܢܰܚܫܘܽܢ: ܣܰܠܡܳܐ: ܒܳܥܳܙ: ܥܘܽܒܶܕ: ܐܝܺܫܰܝ: ܕܰܘܝܺܕ: ܫܠܶܝܡܘܽܢ: ܪܚܰܒܥܰܡ: ܐܰܒܺܝܳܐ: ܐܳܣܳܐ: ܝܘܽܫܳܦܳܛ: ܝܘܽܪܳܡ: ܐܶܚܰܙܝܳܐ: ܝܘܳܐܳܫ: ܐܰܡܨܺܝܳܐ: ܥܘܽܙܺܝܳܐ: ܝܘܳܬܳܡ: ܐܳܚܳܙ: ܚܶܙܩܺܝܳܐ: ܡܢܰܫܶܐ: ܐܳܡܘܽܢ: ܝܘܽܫܺܝܳܐ: ܝܘܳܝܳܩܺܝܡ: ܝܘܳܝܽܟܝܝܢ: ܫܶܠܬܺܝܐܝܠ: ܙܘܽܪܒܿܰܒܶܠ: ܐܰܒܝܽܘܽܕ:
21. Now, however, take the sum of these sixty-three generations from Adam to the birth of Christ: Adam, Seth, Enos, Cainan, Mahalalel, Jared, Enoch, Methuselah, Lamech, Noah, Shem, Arpachshad, Shelah, Eber, Peleg, Reu, Serug, Nahor, Terah, Abraham, Isaac, Jacob, Judah, Perez, Hezron, Ram, Amminadab, Nahshon, Salma, Boaz, Obed, Jesse, David, Solomon, Rehoboam, Abijah, Asa, Jehoshaphat, Jehoram, Ahaziah, Joash, Amaziah, Uzziah, Jotham, Ahaz, Hezekiah, Manasseh, Amon, Josiah, Jehoiakim, Jehoiachin, Shealtiel, Zerubbabel, Abiud, Eliakim, Azor, Zadok, Achim, Eliud,


[p. 66] {#po.0.p66}
ܐܶܠܝܳܩܺܝܡ: ܥܳܙܘܽܪ: ܨܳܕܘܽܩ: ܐܰܟܝܺܢ: ܐܶܠܝܽܘܽܕ݂: ܐܶܠܝܰܥܙܰܪ: ܡܰܬܰܢ: ܝܰܥܩܘܽܒ: ܝܰܘܣܶܦ: ܘܝܶܫܘܽܥ ܒܪܶܗ ܕ݁ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܕܐܶܬܝܺܠܶܕ݂ ܡܶܢ ܡܰܪܝܰܡ
Eleazar, Mathan, Jacob, Joseph, and Jesus, the Son of God, born of the Virgin, whose father was called Joseph.
ܒܬܘܽܠܬܳܐ: ܘܐܶܬܩܪܺܝ ܝܰܘܣܶܦ ܐܰܒܘܽܗ̱ܝ܀ ܡܶܟ݂ܝܺܠ ܐܶܫܰܪܶܐ ܠܰܡܚܰܘܳܝܘܽܬܳܟ݂ ܥܰܠ ܚܘܽܫܒܳܢܳܐ ܕ݁ܰܫܢܰܝܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܐܳܕܳܡ ܒܕ݂ܰܡܳܝܐ ܠܶܗ ܠܗܳܢܳܐ ܚܘܽܫܒܳܢܳܐ ܕܪܶܫܡܶܬ ܘܚܰܘܝܺܬܳܟ. ܐܰܠܳܗܳܐ ܒܪܳܐ ܘܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܳܐܕܳܡ: ܘܟ݁ܰܕ݂ ܗܘܳܐ ܐܳܕܳܡ ܒܰܪ ܡܳܐܐ ܘܰܬܠܳܬܝܺܢ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ ܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܫܶܬ: ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܐܳܕ݁ܳܡ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܕܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܫܶܬ ܬܡܳܢܶܡܳܐܐ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ. ܣܳܟܿܳܐ ܕܰܫܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܬܫܰܥܡܳܐܐ ܘܰܬܠܳܬܝܺܢ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ. ܒܕ݂ܰܡܳܝܐ ܠܰܫܢܰܬ ܬܠܳܬܝܺܢ
22. Therefore, I shall begin to explain to you the calculation of the years from Adam up to the sum indicated and demonstrated to you by me. God created and begot Adam. Now when Adam was one hundred and thirty years old, he begot Seth. And Adam lived after he begot Seth eight hundred years. Therefore, the sum of his years is nine hundred and thirty years; and Adam lived until the thirty-first year of the life of Lamech, the father of Noah, and he died in the ninth generation.
ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܥܕܰܡܳܐ ܠܰܫܢܰܬ ܡܳܐܐ ܘܬܡܳܢܶܝܢ ܕܚܰܝ̈ܘܗ̱ܝ ܕܠܳܡܳܟ. ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܬܪ̈ܶܝܢ ܫܢܺܝܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܕܢܽܘܚ ܢܶܬܺܝܠܶܕ݂: ܘܡܝܬ ܒܕ݂ܳܪܳܐ ܬܡܺܝܢܳܝܳܐ܀ ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܐܶܢܘܽܫ ܬܫܥܺܝܢ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ ܘܰܐܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܩܰܝܢܳܢ: ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܐܶܢܘܽܫ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܕܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܩܰܝܢܳܢ ܬܡܳܢܶܡܳܐܐ ܘܚܰܡܫܰܥܣܪ̈ܶܐ ܫܢܺܝܢ: ܣܳܟܳܐ ܕܰܫܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܬܫܰܥܡܳܐܐ ܘܚܰܡܫܳܐ ܫܢܺܝܢ. ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܐܶܢܘܽܫ ܥܕܰܡܳܐ ܠܰܫܢܰܬ ܫܰܒܥܺܝܢ ܘܬܫܰܥ ܕܚܰܝ̈ܘܗ̱ܝ ܕܢܽܘܚ: ܗܳܝ ܕܺܐܝܬܶܝܗ̱ ܫܢܰܬ ܚܰܡܫܡܳܐܐ ܘܥܶܣܪܺܝܢ ܘܚܕܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܡܰܒܽܘܠܳܐ: ܘܡܝܬ ܒܕ݂ܳܪܳܐ ܬܡܺܝܢܳܝܳܐ܀ ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܩܰܝܢܳܢ ܫܰܒܥܺܝܢ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ ܘܰܐܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܡܰܗܠܳܠܶܐܝܠ: ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܩܰܝܢܳܢ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܕܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܡܰܗܠܳܠܶܐܝܠ ܬܡܳܢܶܡܳܐܐ ܘܐܰܪܒܥܺܝܢ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ: ܣܳܟܳܐ ܕܰܫܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܬܫܰܥܡܳܐܐ
23. Seth also lived one hundred and five years, and begot Enos. And Seth lived after he begot Enos eight hundred and seven years. The sum of his years is nine hundred and twelve years, up to the one hundred and eightieth year of the life of Lamech. And he lived two years before Noah was born, and he died in the eighth generation.


[p. 67] {#po.0.p67}
ܘܥܶܣܪܳܐ ܫܢܺܝܢ. ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܩܰܝܢܳܢ ܥܕܰܡܳܐ ܠܰܫܢܰܬ ܡܳܐܐ ܘܬܫܰܥ ܕܚܰܝ̈ܘܗ̱ܝ ܕܢܽܘܚ: ܗܳܝ ܕܺܐܝܬܶܝܗ̱ ܐܰܪܒܥܡܳܐܐ ܘܥܶܣܪܳܐ ܡܶܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܡܰܒܽܘܠܳܐ: ܘܡܝܬ ܒܕ݂ܳܪܳܐ ܫܒܺܝܥܳܝܳܐ܀ ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܡܰܗܠܳܠܶܐܝܠ ܫܬܺܝܢ ܘܚܰܡܫܳܐ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ ܘܰܐܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܝܳܪܶܕ݂: ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܡܰܗܠܳܠܶܐܝܠ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܕܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܝܳܪܶܕ݂ ܬܡܳܢܶܡܳܐܐ ܘܬܠܳܬܺܝܢ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ: ܣܳܟܳܐ ܕܰܫܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܬܡܳܢܶܡܳܐܐ ܘܬܫܰܥܺܝܢ ܘܚܰܡܫܳܐ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ. ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܡܰܗܠܳܠܶܐܝܠ ܥܕܰܡܳܐ ܠܰܫܢܰܬ ܡܰܐܬܶܝܢ
24. Enos, however, lived ninety years, and begot Cainan. And Enos lived after he begot Cainan eight hundred and fifteen years. The sum of his years is nine hundred and five years. Now Enos lived


[p. 68] {#po.0.p68}
ܘܐܰܪܒܥܰܥܣܪ̈ܶܐ ܕܚܰܝ̈ܘܗ̱ܝ ܕܢܽܘܚ: ܗܳܝ ܕܺܐܝܬܶܝܗ̱ ܬܠܳܬܡܳܐܐ ܘܬܡܳܢܺܝܢ ܘܫܬܳܐ ܡܶܢ
until the seventy-ninth year of the life of Noah, which is the five hundred and twenty-first year before the flood;
ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܡܰܒܽܘܠܳܐ: ܘܡܝܬ ܒܕ݂ܳܪܳܐ ܫܬܺܝܬܳܝܳܐ܀
and he died in the eighth generation.
ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܝܳܪܶܕ݂ ܡܰܐܬܶܝܢ ܘܬܠܳܬܳܐ ܘܫܬܺܝܢ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ ܘܰܐܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܚܢܽܘܟ݂: ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܝܳܪܶܕ݂ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܕܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܚܢܽܘܟ݂ ܬܡܳܢܶܡܳܐܐ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ: ܣܳܟܳܐ ܕܰܫܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܬܫܰܥܡܳܐܐ ܘܫܬܺܝܢ ܘܬܠܳܬܳܐ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ. ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܝܳܪܶܕ݂ ܥܕܰܡܳܐ ܠܰܫܢܰܬ ܬܠܳܬܡܳܐܐ ܘܬܠܳܬܳܐ ܘܬܠܳܬܺܝܢ ܕܚܰܝ̈ܘܗ̱ܝ ܕܢܽܘܚ: ܗܳܝ ܕܺܐܝܬܶܝܗ̱ ܡܰܐܬܶܝܢ ܘܬܠܳܬܳܐ ܘܫܰܒܥܺܝܢ ܡܶܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܡܰܒܽܘܠܳܐ: ܘܡܝܬ ܒܕ݂ܳܪܳܐ ܚܰܡܝܫܳܝܳܐ܀
25. Now Cainan lived seventy years and begot Malaleel. And Cainan lived after he begot Malaleel eight hundred and forty years. The sum of his years is nine hundred and ten years. And Cainan lived until the one hundred and ninth year of the life of Noah, which is the four hundred and tenth before


[p. 69] {#po.0.p69}
ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܚܢܽܘܟ݂ ܫܬܺܝܢ ܘܚܰܡܫܳܐ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ ܘܰܐܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܡܰܬܽܘܫܠܰܚ: ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܚܢܽܘܟ݂ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܕܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܡܰܬܽܘܫܠܰܚ ܬܠܳܬܡܳܐܐ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ: ܣܳܟܳܐ ܕܰܫܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܬܠܳܬܡܳܐܐ
the flood; and he died in the seventh generation.
ܘܫܬܺܝܢ ܘܚܰܡܫܳܐ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ. ܘܰܚܢܽܘܟ݂ ܐܶܬܢܰܣܰܒ݂ ܠܰܐܠܳܗܳܐ: ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܥܕܰܡܳܐ ܠܰܫܢܰܬ ܐܰܪܒܥܡܳܐܐ ܘܬܠܳܬܳܐ ܘܬܠܳܬܺܝܢ ܕܚܰܝ̈ܘܗ̱ܝ ܕܢܽܘܚ: ܗܳܝ ܕܺܐܝܬܶܝܗ̱ ܡܰܐܬܶܝܢ ܘܬܠܳܬܳܐ ܘܫܰܒܥܺܝܢ ܡܶܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܡܰܒܽܘܠܳܐ: ܘܡܝܬ ܒܕ݂ܳܪܳܐ ܪܒܺܝܥܳܝܳܐ܀ ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܡܰܬܽܘܫܠܰܚ ܫܰܒܥܺܝܢ ܘܬܫܰܥ ܘܡܰܐܐ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ ܘܰܐܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܠܳܡܳܟ: ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܡܰܬܽܘܫܠܰܚ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܕܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܠܳܡܳܟ ܫܰܒܥܡܳܐܐ ܘܬܡܳܢܺܝܢ ܘܬܠܳܬܳܐ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ: ܣܳܟܳܐ ܕܰܫܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܬܫܰܥܡܳܐܐ ܘܬܫܰܥ ܘܫܬܺܝܢ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ. ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܡܰܬܽܘܫܠܰܚ ܥܕܰܡܳܐ ܠܰܫܢܰܬ ܚܰܡܫܡܳܐܐ ܘܬܫܰܥ ܘܬܫܰܥܺܝܢ ܕܚܰܝ̈ܘܗ̱ܝ ܕܢܽܘܚ: ܗܳܝ ܕܺܐܝܬܶܝܗ̱ ܚܰܕ݂ܥܶܣܪ̈ܶܐ ܫܢ̈ܝܺܢ ܡܶܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܡܰܒܽܘܠܳܐ: ܘܡܝܬ ܒܕ݂ܳܪܳܐ ܬܠܺܝܬܳܝܳܐ܀
26. Malaleel, however, lived sixty-five years, and begot Jared. And Malaleel lived after he begot Jared eight hundred and thirty years. The sum of his years is eight hundred and ninety-five years. And Malaleel lived until the two hundred and fourteenth year of the life of Noah, which is the three hundred and eighty-sixth before the flood; and he died in the sixth generation.
ܘܐܰܪܒ݁ܰܥܣܪ̈ܶܝܢ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ ܠܚܰܝ̈ܘܗܝ ܕܢܘܽܚ: ܗܳܢܰܘ ܥܕ݂ܰܡܳܐ ܠܰܫܢܰܬ ܬܠܳܬܡܳܐܐ ܘܰܬܡܳܢܝܢ ܘܰܬܪ̈ܬܶܝܢ ܡܶܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ ܡܰܒܽܘܠܳܐ: ܘܡܝܬ ܒ݁ܕ݂ܳܪܳܐ ܚܡܝܺܫܳܝܳܐ܀ ܘܰܚܝܳܐ ܚܢܘܽܟ ܫܬܝܺܢ ܘܰܚܡܶܫ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ ܘܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܡܰܬܘܽܫܠܰܚ: ܘܰܫܦܰܪ ܚܢܘܽܟ ܡܶܢ ܒܳܬܰܪ ܕܐܰܘܠܶܕ݂ ܠܡܰܬܘܽܫܠܰܚ ܬܠܳܬܡܳܐܐ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ. ܣܳܟ݁ܳܐ ܕܰܫܢܰܘ̈ܗ̱ܝ ܬܠܳܬܡܳܐܐ ܘܰܫܬܝܺܢ ܘܰܚܡܶܫ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ. ܘܒܰܫܢܰܬ ܬܠܳܬܡܳܐܐ ܕܡܰܬܘܽܫܠܰܚ
27. And Jared lived one hundred and sixty-two years and begot Enoch. Now Jared lived after he begot Enoch eight hundred years. The sum of his years is nine hundred and sixty-two years. And Jared lived until the three hundred and
ܒܪܶܗ: ܘܒܰܫܢܰܬ ܡܳܐܐ ܘܰܬܠܳܬܰܥܣܪ̈ܶܐ ܠܚܰܝ̈ܘܗܝ
seventy-eighth year of the life of Noah, that is, until the two hundred and twenty-second year before the flood; and
ܕܠܳܡܳܟ: ܫܬܝܺܢ ܘܰܬܫܰܥ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ ܡܶܢ ܩܕ݂ܳܡ
he died in the fifth generation.
